# Estate OS: product specification

**Version:** 1.0 (consolidated), 2026-10-08.
**Inputs:** `01-market-research.md`, `02-plan.md`, `mobbin-ui-teardown.md`, `mobbin-ui-teardown-2-portal.md`, and the eight benchmark files in `features/` (01 buyer portal, 02 seller and listing, 03 agent CRM, 04 transactions and back office, 05 property management, 06 developers and international, 07 platform core, 08 ecosystem and pain points).
**What this document is:** the single product spec for Estate OS. Every capability benchmarked in the feature files appears in exactly one home module below. Duplicates found in several files (e-sign, inbox, payments, consent, offers, reporting, MCP and so on) were merged into one home row; the merged rows keep their full text under **Also** and their IDs in brackets, so nothing was dropped. Source URLs are copied unchanged from the feature files. Numbers are the vendors' or regulators' own figures as quoted there; claims the research could not confirm are listed in section 6.

**How to read the tables.** Each feature row has: ID (traceable to the feature file), Feature, What it does, Priority (Must / Should / Later, with the qualifier from the feature file, such as "Must (UK)"), Benchmark leader (the vendor or rule that sets the bar, with the evidence text), and Source (the URLs). Priority keys follow the feature files: **Must** = needed at launch to be "not weaker than the leader" for that persona or country; **Should** = within the first year; **Later** = differentiator, enterprise or niche. A qualifier like "(UK)" or "(STR)" means the row is a Must only for that country or asset pack.

**Contents**

1. One-page summary
2. Module-by-module specification (13 modules)
3. Compliance packs by country and state
4. Core data model
5. Release plan
6. Open questions and not-verified list
7. Count summary

---


## 1. One-page summary

### Who it is for

Estate OS is one product for every business that touches a home, sold worldwide with the US first and packs for the UK, UAE, India, Australia and Canada:

- **Agents and teams** who need a website, CRM, inbox, AI follow-up and a deal file.
- **Brokerages and franchises** who also need compliance review, commissions, trust accounting, recruiting and reporting.
- **Property managers and landlords** running long-term rentals, short-term rentals, HOAs and commercial space, with portals for tenants, owners, vendors, guests and boards.
- **Developers and builders** running launches, live inventory, payment plans, channel partners, construction-linked demands, handover and warranty.
- **Consumers as portal users:** buyers, co-buyers, sellers, homeowners, tenants, owners, guests, channel partners and vendors. They never buy Estate OS; they are invited by the business that does.

### The bundle model: turn modules on, never accept a weaker module

The market is five silos (agent CRM, transactions/back office, property management, developer software, regional broker CRMs) that do not share data (`01-market-research.md`). The one vendor that went wide natively, BoldTrail, averages 1.46 stars in recent App Store reviews. So the rule for Estate OS is: **a customer turns on only the modules it needs, and every module must match the segment leader row for row.** The module tables in section 2 are that bar. All modules read and write one graph of Person/Household, Property/Unit, Listing, Deal, Lease, Project, Ledger and Conversation, so turning on a second module never means a second database.

| Persona bundle | Modules turned on | Leader to match | First release that fully covers it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Solo agent | 1 Website & search, 4 CRM, 5 Inbox, 6 AI copilot, 7 Transactions (lite e-sign), 13 Core | Follow Up Boss Grow, Wise Agent | MVP waves 1–3 |
| Team | Solo + routing and ponds, AI ISA, leaderboards, offers, 2 Buyer portal, 3 Seller portal | FUB Pro, Lofty, Ylopo | MVP waves 1–3 |
| Brokerage / franchise | Team + 7 full compliance review, 8 Back office, 12 Reporting, recruiting, LMS, intranet, franchise org tree | BoldTrail + BackOffice + Recruit, SkySlope, Paperless Pipeline, Lone Wolf | MVP wave 4 |
| Transaction coordinator | 7 Transactions (per-file pricing), 6 contract AI, 5 Inbox | ListedKit, Open to Close, SkySlope | MVP wave 3 |
| Property manager / landlord | 9 Property management (LTR) with tenant, owner and vendor portals; 5 Inbox; 6 PM agents | AppFolio, Buildium, DoorLoop, RentRedi (DIY) | MVP wave 5 |
| Short-term rental operator | 9 STR pack (channel manager, calendar, cleaning, owner app) | Guesty, Hostaway | MVP wave 7 (STR pack) |
| HOA / association manager | 9 HOA pack (dues, violations, ARC, board portal) | Buildium, AppFolio, Vantaca | MVP wave 7 (HOA pack) |
| Developer / builder | 10 Developer, 4 CRM, 5 Inbox (WhatsApp, masked calls), 11 Marketing; CP portal | Sell.Do + IRIS, Farvision (India); Lasso + Zonda + Hyphen (US) | MVP wave 6 |
| Regional broker (UK, UAE, AU, IN, CA) | 4 CRM, 3 listings with portal syndication, 8 client accounts, country pack | Reapit, PropSpace, Goyzer, MRI Vault, Rex | MVP wave 8 (country packs) |

Commercial terms come from the churn evidence: published prices, month-to-month, self-serve cancel, agent data that leaves with the agent, metered usage shown live, and every tenant fee shown before payment (`01-market-research.md`; `07-platform-core.md` §17).

### The 12 sharpest differentiators and the evidence behind each

1. **One record from lead to buyer to homeowner to landlord to investor.** Shared Person, Household, Property, Listing, Deal, Lease, Project and Ledger across every module. Evidence: SkySlope users keep "two file folders for every transaction" and FUB mobile cannot search a related contact ([FUB App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id966131499)) (`01-market-research.md`); back offices sync statuses only once and by hand ([SkySlope Books mapping](https://bookssupport.skyslope.com/support/solutions/articles/158000122019-skyslope-field-and-status-mapping), `features/04`); no seller-side vendor keeps one Listing record across CMA, showings, offers and nurture (`features/02`); brokers ask for "one user interface", not one more dashboard ([RealEstateNews on T3 Sixty](https://www.realestatenews.com/2026/10/05/what-do-brokers-really-want-from-tech-one-simple-interface), `features/08` B1).
2. **Full mobile parity with offline capture and sessions that stay signed in.** Evidence: recent written-review averages of 1.46 (BoldTrail), 1.45 (Lofty), 1.54 (SkySlope), 1.48 (Buildium) (`01-market-research.md`); "No Digisign?" in the SkySlope app ([App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id1608944544), `features/04`); AppFolio's staff app crashes after photos and logs out mid-emergency ([App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id1057763031), `features/05`).
3. **One inbox that logs everything, with native WhatsApp, Instagram, Messenger and RCS, international calling, and the agent's own phone.** Evidence: FUB lists WhatsApp and Messenger only as "being considered" ([FUB Inbox Apps](https://help.followupboss.com/hc/en-us/articles/23459816940055-Inbox-Apps-Overview)) and cannot call or text outside the US and Canada ([FUB International](https://help.followupboss.com/hc/en-us/articles/10321054750487-International-Calling-and-Texting)) (`features/03`); WhatsApp is standard in Sell.Do and Hostaway ([Sell.Do](https://www.sell.do/real-estate-crm/communication-hub), [Hostaway](https://www.hostaway.com/features/communication/)).
4. **Settlement-native deals.** A buyer agreement that gates tours, a compensation card, compensation carried into the CDA with a cap check, and a seller net sheet with concession and buyer-broker lines. Evidence: written agreement before touring with compensation "clearly defined" ([NAR consumer guide](https://www.nar.realtor/the-facts/consumer-guide-to-written-buyer-agreements), `features/04`); Sierra handles this only as a tag recipe ([Sierra BAA workflow](https://help.sierrainteractive.com/helpcenter/set-up-a-buyers-agency-agreement-tracking-workflow), `features/03`); no vendor checks the compensation cap at CDA time (TX-18.9, `features/04`); MLS compensation fields were removed ([OneKey](https://support.onekeymls.com/hc/en-us/articles/28842130579988-NAR-Settlement-Compensation-Fields-to-be-Removed-from-the-MLS), `features/02`).
5. **Listing-policy compliance engine inside the agent's workflow.** Clear Cooperation clock fed by a public-marketing log, office-exclusive and delayed-marketing certifications, Zillow "broadly accessible" guard, per-MLS photo and AI-staging rules, IDX AVM opt-outs and exclusion disclosures. Evidence: [NAR Multiple Listing Options for Sellers](https://www.nar.realtor/about-nar/policies/multiple-listing-options-for-sellers); 48 bans and 1,202 violation notices under Zillow's standards ([Mike DelPrete](https://www.mikedp.com/articles/2025/11/25/zillows-listing-ban-and-violation-metrics), `features/07`); MRED exclusion rule ([MLS Grid](https://docs.mlsgrid.com/recent-releases/mred-idx-and-vow-rules-changes.md)).
6. **A consent ledger in the core.** One gate for SMS, voice, AI voice, email and WhatsApp covering TCPA consent and revocation "in any reasonable manner", 10DLC, CAN-SPAM, CCPA/GPC and India's DPDP. Evidence: [FCC](https://www.fcc.gov/consumers/guides/stop-unwanted-robocalls-and-texts) and [Federal Register revocation rule](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2024/03/05/2024-04587/strengthening-the-ability-of-consumers-to-stop-robocalls) (`features/03`, `features/07`); Lofty's consent-status model ([Lofty TCPA Guide](https://help.lofty.com/hc/en-us/articles/32764090351899-Telephone-Consumer-Protection-Act-TCPA-Guide)); consumers spammed after one valuation request ([Trustpilot HomeLight](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/homelight.com), `features/08` B11).
7. **AI that acts, cites its source, asks before it changes things, and never blocks the manual path.** Every AI-filled field shows where it came from; every AI action has an autonomy level and a ledger entry; every AI intake has a "use the form" exit. Evidence: Paperless Pipeline hover-to-source ([What's New](https://help.paperlesspipeline.com/help/-whats-new)) and ListedKit Approve/Dismiss/Always approve ([Ava Actions](https://help.listedkit.com/en/articles/14847827-ava-actions-approving-what-ava-proposes)) (`features/04`); FolioSpace residents forced into an AI chat ("give me the choice", [App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id1376350358), `features/05`); only ≤10% of agents use AI for transaction work ([NAR](https://www.nar.realtor/news/real-estate-news/technology/the-tech-real-estate-agents-rely-on-most-in-2026), `features/08` B7).
8. **Wire-safe money movement and a consumer closing command center.** Wiring instructions only inside the portal, callback verification, fraud education at each money step, and one timeline with a named owner per closing step. Evidence: real estate fraud of $275.1M across 12,368 complaints in 2025 ([HousingWire](https://www.housingwire.com/articles/fbi-cybercrime-losses-real-estate-fraud-hits-275m/), `features/08`); Qualia Connect's buyer app rated 2.5 stars ("no option to create an account", [App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/qualia-connect/id1279573162), `features/01`); Qualia Shield and CertifID sell protection only to title companies ([Qualia Shield](https://www.qualia.com/shield/), [CertifID](https://www.certifid.com/)).
9. **Trust-first commercials and portable data.** Published prices, month-to-month, in-app cancel, live usage meters, an ownership stamp on every record, a one-click agent-departure flow, open-format backups, and a fee shown to every tenant before they pay with a free ACH path. Evidence: kvCORE "refused to cancel my subscription service which was 1500 per month" after a brokerage switch ([App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id1304578287)); Rentec gives one free ACH per unit per month ([Rentec FAQ](https://www.rentecdirect.com/details/easypay-faq), `features/05`); FTC rental-fee rulemaking ([FTC](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2026/03/ftc-seeks-public-comment-proposed-rulemaking-regarding-unfair-or-deceptive-rental-housing-fee), `features/08`); PropSpace publishes AED 220/user ([PropSpace pricing](https://www.propspace.com/pricing), `features/06`).
10. **One ledger and one owner app across long-term, short-term, HOA and commercial.** Trust-compliant double entry with three-way reconciliation and per-owner sub-ledgers. Evidence: managers running STR and LTR buy Guesty or Hostaway alongside AppFolio or Buildium ([Hostaway owner portal](https://www.hostaway.com/features/owner-portal/), [AppFolio accounting](https://www.appfolio.com/property-manager/accounting-reporting), `features/05`); Owners by Guesty averages 1.08 stars in recent reviews ([App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id6749070952)).
11. **One inventory engine for launches, builder site maps, rental vacancy and short-let calendars.** Holds with their own expiry clock, a concurrency guard, a provable fair queue, brokerage released only when the buyer pays, and demands raised by site certification. Evidence: Sell.Do IRIS "Two agents opened B-1204 together. One booking won." ([IRIS](https://www.sell.do/products/iris/inventory-bookings-documentation)) and the fair queue ([IRIS queue](https://www.sell.do/products/iris/queue-management)); Farvision collection-linked brokerage and site-certified demands ([Farvision Care](https://www.farvisionerp.com/farvision-module-crm-care.html)) (`features/06`). No US equivalent exists (`01-market-research.md`).
12. **Country rule packs on one core, plus an open agent layer.** UK RRA, deposit protection and NHQC timers; Dubai Trakheesi permits and Madmoun QR; India RERA, GST/TDS and DPDP; Victoria and NSW off-the-plan rules; Ontario offer rules; all on the same records, with an MCP server and API that inherit the user's permissions on every tier. Evidence: [GOV.UK RRA guide](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/guide-to-the-renters-rights-act/guide-to-the-renters-rights-act); [DLD Madmoun](https://dubailand.gov.ae/en/news-media/dubai-land-department-provides-madmoun-service-to-verify-validity-of-real-estate-ads-via-qr-codes); [Consumer Affairs Victoria](https://www.consumer.vic.gov.au/housing/buying-and-selling-property/buying-property/buying-off-the-plan) (`features/06`); Rechat's MCP keeps the user's permissions ([Rechat help](https://help.rechat.com/appendix/integrations/connect-rechat-to-your-ai-assistant), `features/07`) while AppFolio keeps read/write API for its top tier ([AppFolio pricing](https://www.appfolio.com/pricing), `features/05`).

### Design direction (from the Mobbin teardowns)

- **Public site and buyer portal:** Zillow-style split map/list search with price pins; listing page with a photo mosaic, the monthly cost directly under the price, and a tour-first CTA showing the next available slots; a budget badge on every card; a six-stage journey home (Get ready → Find → Tour → Offer → Under contract → Close) with "your next step" and a to-do list; an Opendoor-style offer stepper; a closing guide with a progress bar; a homeowner dashboard with a proceeds calculator.
- **Operator side:** Attio/Twenty density with thin borders and record pages (timeline, tasks, emails, notes); HoneyBook-style "Today" home with counters; Zillow Rentals property tabs (Overview · Listing · Applications · Leases · Payments · Maintenance) with a "Next steps" checklist; DocuSign/PandaDoc signing pattern ("N fields remaining"); Calendly-style two-pane schedulers with round-robin pools.
- **Cross-industry:** milestone trackers with an owner and status per step, setup checklists with "2/5" progress, Wise-style money-movement step lists with a verified-recipient badge.

## 2. Module-by-module specification


### 2.0 Rules that apply to every module

These rules are stated once here and referenced from every module.

1. **One home per capability.** Shared capabilities live in one module and other modules call them. The map:

| Capability | Home module | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| E-signature (envelopes, signer order, certificates, mobile signing) | 7 Transactions (§7.4) | Buyer portal, seller portal, leases (9), developer bookings (10), agent onboarding (8) |
| Conversations, inbox, SMS, WhatsApp, email, calls | 5 Inbox | Every module; consumer deal threads appear inside the portals |
| Consent, DNC, quiet hours, opt-outs, 10DLC | 5 Inbox (§5.4), on the ConsentRecord object in Core | CRM automations, AI ISA, marketing, PM, developer drips |
| Money in (ACH, card, wallets, cash, gateways) and payouts | Payment rails in Core (billing/metering, PCI scope) with ledgers in 8 (brokerage trust), 9 (PM trust) and 10 (developer collections) | EMD (8), rent (9), tokens and demands (10), agent billing (8) |
| Wire-fraud protection | 2 Buyer portal (§2.5, consumer flow) and 7 (§7.11, brokerage controls) | All closings |
| Offers | 3 Seller portal (§3.11, listing side) and 2 Buyer portal (§2.4, buyer side); the accepted offer becomes the deal in 7 (§7.1) | CRM pipeline |
| Showings and tours | 3 (§3.8, listing side), 2 (§2.3, buyer side), 9 (§9.3, rentals), 10 (§10.4, site visits) on one scheduling engine | CRM calendar |
| Reporting engine and dashboards | 12 Reporting | Every module's KPIs |
| Workflow builder and AI agents | 6 AI layer | CRM plans (4) are a specialised view of the same engine |
| Documents, storage, retention | 13 Core (§13.4) with transaction documents in 7 (§7.2) | All |
| Inventory (units, holds, availability) | 10 Developer (§10.1) as the engine; rental vacancy (9.2) and STR calendar (9.23) read it | Website (1) |

2. **Mobile parity.** Every Must row ships on web, iOS and Android, including iPad layout, before it counts as shipped (13.10).
3. **AI human-in-the-loop rule (applies to every "AI features" list below).** Each AI capability has an autonomy level set by an admin: *suggest → draft → act with approval → autonomous* (PC-21.2). Defaults are conservative: anything that sends to a consumer, changes money, changes a price, changes a legal date or files a compliance decision is at most "act with approval" until an admin raises it. Every AI-filled field shows its source; every AI action is written to the AI action ledger with prompt, tools, model, user and approver (PC-21.1). Every AI intake (chat, voice, triage) has a visible form or human path next to it. AI identity is disclosed in consumer messages, and AI outreach runs only after a consent check (PC-21.5).
4. **Permissions.** Record visibility = role scope (own / team / office / org / all) ∪ share grants ∪ public display rules; field masks hide commission, IDs, bank and screening data from roles that do not need them; external portal users are roles in the same engine (13.2). AI, API, Zapier and MCP actors carry `on_behalf_of_user_id` and can never exceed that user.
5. **Notifications.** All notifications go through one notification service that deep-links to the exact record, respects per-user channel preferences and quiet hours, and can mark compliance notices as mandatory (13.11).
6. **Audit.** Every create, update, delete, export, permission change, login and AI action is in the append-only audit log, with field history on regulated fields (13.4).


### Module 1. Website, IDX and consumer search

**Rows:** 68 (29 Must, 28 Should, 11 Later).

**Purpose.** The public front door: brokerage, office, agent and single-property websites, and the consumer search experience (map, filters, alerts, listing pages, neighborhood data) that the logged-in buyer portal (Module 2) reuses. It must match Zillow, Redfin, Realtor.com, Domain and Rightmove on search, and Lofty, Real Geeks, Luxury Presence and IDX Broker on agent sites, while obeying every MLS display rule (13.6).

**Users.** Anonymous visitors; registered consumers (buyers, renters, homeowners); agents and marketing staff who edit sites; brokerage admins who set brand and compliance; the system (IDX feeds, display-rule engine).

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PC-16.1 | Brokerage site + agent subsites + listing single-property sites, from one CMS | Roster, bios and office pages come from the org graph. IDX search is native | Must | IDX Broker rosters, bio pages, lead routing (IDX Broker); Rechat Lucy builds listing sites "within seconds" (Rechat) | [IDX Broker](https://www.idxbroker.com/) ; [Rechat](https://rechat.ai/) |
| PC-16.2 | AI site builder with approve-before-publish | AI drafts and edits from chat. Nothing goes live without approval | Should | Placester: "AI builds your complete… website in under 4 minutes… Nothing goes live until you say so" (Placester); Luxury Presence "You approve what goes live" (Luxury Presence) | [Placester](https://placester.com/) ; [Luxury Presence](https://www.luxurypresence.com/) |

Applies to governments; private brokerages face Title III litigation (not sourced) | [ADA.gov](https://www.ada.gov/resources/2024-03-08-web-rule/) |

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B17 | **Hidden ownership costs** | Buyers | 45% say homeownership costs more than expected; 58% "could not accurately estimate repair costs before buying"; 63% deferred maintenance ([Clever 2026](https://listwithclever.com/research/costs-of-owning-a-home-2026/)) | "True monthly cost" (PITI + HOA + insurance quote + maintenance reserve) on every listing and offer; post-close maintenance plan. |
| B29 | **Rental listing scams with no way to report** | Renters, landlords | "There is no good way to report listings that are fraudulent" ([App Store Zillow](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id310738695), 2026-10-03); "3 rental scams in less than a week" ([App Store Redfin](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id327962480), 2026-09-14); landlord: "All of the requests I have had so far are phishing and there is no way in messages to report" ([App Store id759202892](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id759202892)) | Verified-owner badge (deed or PM verification), a one-tap report on every listing and message, and link scanning in inbound inquiries. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Visitor to registered lead.** Visitor searches (location typeahead, map, draw, filters) → saves a home or search → registration with consent text naming the business (5.4) → a Person record is created or matched (4.4) → lead event typed "Registration" or "Saved Property Search" enters routing (4.2) → saved-search alerts start with the visitor's chosen frequency.
2. **Listing page to tour.** Listing page shows photo mosaic, price, monthly cost line (P&I, tax, insurance, HOA, PMI, utilities, insurance quote estimate) and the next available tour slots → visitor taps a slot → if a buyer agreement or ID check is required (2.3, BP-T13) the gate opens inline → request lands in the inbox and the agent's calendar as "Requested", never "Confirmed" until confirmed.
3. **Saved-search alert.** New or changed listing arrives from the feed (13.6) → matcher runs saved searches (including drawn, multi-area and school searches) → alert sent per the search's channel and frequency (instant, daily, off) → "homes you might like" suppressed separately → the click is tracked on the lead timeline (4.9).
4. **Agent site launch.** Agent or brokerage picks a theme → roster, bios and office pages are filled from the org graph (13.1) → IDX search widgets placed → compliance auto-insert adds brokerage name, licence numbers, Equal Housing logo and MLS attribution (11.3) → AI site edits are drafted and published only after approval → custom domain, SSL and redirects from the old vendor's URLs.
5. **Natural-language search.** Visitor types "3-bed with a driveway near Lincoln Elementary under 800k" → LLM turns it into structured filters plus semantic matches, shown as editable chips → fair-housing guard removes steering terms → results honor the filters strictly (BP-D16).

#### States and statuses

- **Listing display status** (from the feed, plain-language glossary BP-D25): Coming Soon / Preview, Active, Active Kick-out, Contingent, Pending, Pending-Taking Backups, Sold, Off-market; plus exempt states (office exclusive, delayed marketing) that are never shown publicly until allowed (3.5).
- **Saved search:** active · paused · deleted; per-channel frequency instant / daily / off.
- **Site page:** draft · pending approval · published · archived.
- **Visitor:** anonymous → registered → VOW-qualified (accepted terms, linked to an agent; can see sold data where the MLS allows).

#### Notifications

Saved-search matches; price drop, pending, back on market, open house and sold events on saved homes (BP-D19); off-market and coming-soon alerts where allowed (BP-D22); agent-picks posted to the consumer feed (BP-D21); to the agent: registration, repeat views, saves and showing requests (via 4.9 smart notifications).

#### Permissions

Public pages show only data allowed by the display-rule set for that MLS, feed type and surface (13.6). VOW data and sold data require login and terms acceptance. Brokerage-exclusive inventory (BP-D24) shows only to logged-in clients of that brokerage. Agents edit their own subsite; office admins edit office pages; brand locks (11.2) cannot be overridden by agents.

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

Natural-language and image search (BP-D10, BP-D11); conversational listing assistant answering from listing data with citations and a hand-off to a person (BP-D12); AI virtual staging initiated by the buyer, always labelled and paired with the original (BP-D51); AI site builder that drafts but publishes only on approval (PC-16.2). None of these can hide the plain filter panel or the "contact a person" path.

#### Compliance hooks

MLS display rules, attribution, refresh limits and "Some listings have been excluded from this website" disclosure (13.6, PC-7.3); seller opt-outs for address, internet display, AVM and comments (3.5); fair-housing-safe neighborhood data, no demographic steering (BP-D76); school-zone disclaimer (BP-D69); UK material information and EPC (BP-D68); WCAG 2.2 AA templates (PC-16.6); verified-listing badges and scam reporting (BP-D27, pain point B29); TCPA-grade consent on every form (5.4); CCPA "Do Not Sell or Share" and GPC (13.13).

#### Integrations

RESO Web API and aggregators (Bridge, Trestle, Spark, MLS Grid) via 13.6; AVM licence (BP-D55); GreatSchools/Niche, Walk Score, flood/noise/risk layers, parcel data; Matterport, Zillow 3D, CubiCasa media; Google Maps/Street View; schema.org `RealEstateListing`; WordPress IDX plugin; branded consumer app (CRM-19.7).


### Module 2. Buyer and homeowner portal

**Rows:** 147 (87 Must, 41 Should, 19 Later).

**Purpose.** A white-label buyer hub that runs from first search to keys and then becomes the homeowner's dashboard, owned by the agent's brand. Zillow launched the only comparable consumer hub in June 2026 and routes it to its own agents and loans; Compass One is limited to Compass (`features/01`). Estate OS gives any brokerage the same arc on the shared Deal and Property records.

**Users.** Buyers, co-buyers and scoped guests (parent giving gift funds, attorney, relocation manager); the buyer's agent, TC, lender, title/escrow officer, inspector and insurer as deal-team members; homeowners after closing; investors evaluating a property.

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

| BP-C1 (+ECO-A2.2, CRM-8.7) | Shared milestone timeline | Contract-derived dates (EMD, inspection, appraisal, loan approval, final walk-through, closing) with countdowns, visible to buyer and team <br>**Also (ECO-A2.2, Closing tracker visible to the consumer):** A plain-language timeline covering title commitment, payoff, CD, signing and funding, with a named owner for each step. <br>**Also (CRM-8.7, Transaction portal for clients):** A client-facing transaction portal. <br>**Regulatory note:** None | Must | Compass One timeline "upcoming steps, recent updates, and pending tasks" (Compass One); Qualia progress tracker (Qualia Connect) · Zillow seller on Trustpilot: "a title and escrow company was selected without me feeling that I had been properly consulted… lack of clear communication regarding… the inspection, the appraisal" (Trustpilot Zillow) · Lofty Transaction Lead Portal | [Compass One](https://one.compass.com/) ; [Qualia Connect](https://www.qualia.com/connect-for-title-and-escrow/) ; [Trustpilot Zillow](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/zillow.com) ; [Transaction Lead Portal](https://help.lofty.com/hc/en-us/articles/48972164737435-Transaction-Lead-Portal) |

| BP-C13 (+ECO-A2.4) | Wire-fraud education at contract start | Up-front warning: wiring instructions never change by email; call a known number <br>**Also (ECO-A2.4, Wire-fraud education baked into the timeline):** Automatic warnings at contract, CD and funding steps, with a phone number taken from the verified directory rather than from email. <br>**Regulatory note:** None | Must | Qualia Shield "educate your buyers… the moment the transaction begins" (Qualia Shield) · YouTube titles show the consumer fear: "Couple wires $800,000 home down payment to scammer", "Confession: How I Lost A $52,660 Home Down Payment To Scammer" (YouTube search) | [Qualia Shield](https://www.qualia.com/shield/) ; [YouTube search](https://www.youtube.com/results?search_query=wire+fraud+home+closing+lost+down+payment) |

Pair this with in-app messaging so wiring instructions never sit in email. | Must | Qualia Shield secure, encrypted channel instead of email (Qualia Shield) · CertifID (acquired Closinglock, per certifid.com). 52% of consumers were "not aware" or only "somewhat aware" of wire fraud risk. | BP-P1 (+SL-14.1) | Automatic homeowner dashboard | Closing turns the deal into a tracked home: value, equity and documents, with no re-entry <br>**Also (SL-14.1, Homeowner dashboard on the agent's domain):** Value, equity, comps and neighbourhood activity for one or more properties; monthly emails bring the owner back | Must | Redfin Owner Dashboard (Redfin); Zoopla My Home (Zoopla) · Luxury Presence | [Redfin](https://support.redfin.com/hc/en-us/articles/360001454251-Finding-the-Deal-Room-and-Owner-Dashboard) ; [Zoopla](https://help.zoopla.co.uk/hc/en-gb/articles/360018826778-What-is-My-Home) ; https://www.luxurypresence.com/blogs/product-update-homeowner-dashboard-home-value-alerts/ |
| BP-P2 | Value tracking with "tune value" | Monthly AVM; owner submits comps or an appraisal to adjust it; corrects home facts | Must | Homebot Tune Value (Homebot); Redfin Edit Home Facts (Redfin) | [Homebot](https://homebot.ai/blog/what-is-a-home-report-comparing-the-top-platforms-for-loan-officers-and-real-estate-agents) ; [Redfin](https://support.redfin.com/hc/en-us/articles/360001433172-Edit-Your-Home-Facts) |
| BP-P3 | Equity tracker | Equity in $ and %, month over month, from purchase price and loan balance | Must | Homebot (Homebot); Rocket "track your equity growth" (Rocket app); Zoopla Mortgage & Equity tab (Zoopla) | [Homebot](https://homebot.ai/blog/what-is-a-home-report-comparing-the-top-platforms-for-loan-officers-and-real-estate-agents) ; [Rocket app](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/rocket-mortgage/id431156417) ; [Zoopla](https://help.zoopla.co.uk/hc/en-gb/articles/360018826778-What-is-My-Home) |
| BP-P4 | Refi / cash-out alerts | Triggered when rates and the owner's loan line up, with an honest "not worth it" answer when it is not | Should | Homebot moment-based emails and Homebot AI (Homebot Q2 2026) | [Homebot Q2 2026](https://homebot.ai/blog/q2-2026-product-releases) |

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B14 | **Sellers can't see who owns which closing step; asked to sign without review** | Sellers | "expected to sign important legal documents without having had a proper opportunity to review and understand them beforehand" (same source) | Consumer closing portal: every document posted 72h ahead, with plain-language summaries and a named owner per step. |
| B19 | **Wire fraud awareness gap** | Buyers, sellers | 52% not or only somewhat aware; 35% of first-time buyers think protecting them is "their real estate agent's responsibility" ([CertifID](https://www.certifid.com/article/state-of-wire-fraud-2025-what-consumers-want-real-estate-professionals-to-know)) | In-app verified wire flow plus automatic agent-branded fraud warnings (A2). |
| B20 | **Title portals forced on consumers are broken** | Buyers, sellers | "Forced to use this by title company. No options to save as you go"; "the only support option available is email" ([App Store id1279573162](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id1279573162)) | Title intake forms inside the same consumer app the agent already invited them to, with autosave. |
| B35 | **Concierge and moving apps fail or upsell** | Buyers, tenants | See A5 quotes ([Trustpilot Updater](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/updater.com)) | A5 no-upsell checklist. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Onboarding.** Agent invites by SMS or email (magic link, no "organization name" field, BP-C10) → buyer answers intent question (buying, selling, both, browsing) and profile intake (first-time buyer, timeline, must-haves) → the hub shows the six-stage journey (Get ready → Find → Tour → Offer → Under contract → Close) with "your next step".
2. **Budget and pre-approval.** Buyer enters income, debts, savings and preferred payment → two numbers: target price and max price, refreshed with live rates → every listing card gets "In budget / Stretch / Over" → buyer uploads any lender's letter (or starts a soft-pull pre-qual with a partner, with RESPA affiliated-business disclosure and a neutral lender list) → agent reviews → the pre-approved flag can be shared with listing agents.
3. **Buyer agreement and first tour.** Buyer taps a tour slot → if no agreement is on file, the state-specific touring agreement opens with a compensation card (agent fee, what the seller offers, possible gap) and a plain-language explainer → buyer signs on the phone or chooses "sign at the home" → tour request goes to "Requested" → agent confirms → itinerary, route, calendar sync, offline access codes → after the visit the buyer rates the home and notes feed the shared board and the agent. Later the touring agreement upgrades to the exclusive agreement without re-entering data (BP-R20; the agreement object lives in 7.9).
4. **Offer.** "Start an offer" on the listing → stepper: price with live payment and cash-to-close, earnest money, financing, contingencies with day counts, dates, concessions and buyer-agent compensation request, inclusions → plain-language summary ("you'll owe $X at signing; you can walk away until day 10") → e-sign by every co-buyer in order on their own phones → status tracker (drafted, sent, viewed, countered, accepted, rejected, expired) → seller counters arrive as a redline.
5. **Under contract to close.** Accepted offer creates the Deal (7.1) → contract dates become the shared milestone timeline with per-party tasks → typed document requests with save-as-you-go forms → EMD paid by ACH to escrow with receipt → wire-fraud education at contract start → inspection booked inside the window, report lands in the vault, repair-request builder → appraisal and loan milestones (LOS sync) → title status, HOA docs, insurance quotes and binder → Closing Disclosure review three business days before closing with a consent toggle for sharing → cash-to-close and verified wiring instructions confirmed by callback → walk-through checklist → closing appointment (in person, hybrid or RON) → keys and a celebration card.
6. **Becoming a homeowner.** Closing turns the Deal into a tracked home with value (tune-able AVM), equity, documents archive, monthly digest, refi alerts that may honestly say "not worth it", move-up buying power, rent-vs-sell estimate (hands off to Module 9 if the owner rents it out), referral and review prompts routed to the same agent.

#### States and statuses

- **Journey stage:** Get ready · Find · Tour · Offer · Under contract · Close · Homeowner. Advances on events (pre-approval verified, offer accepted, cleared to close) without agent clicks (BP-C2).
- **Tour:** requested · confirmed · rescheduled · done · cancelled (BP-T18); "requested" is never shown as confirmed (BP-T4).
- **Offer:** drafted · sent · viewed by listing agent · countered · accepted · rejected · expired · backup (BP-O14, BP-O18).
- **Buyer agreement:** none · touring (non-exclusive, with expiry) · exclusive · terminated (detail in 7.9).
- **Task (per party):** open · in progress · done; document request: requested · uploaded · accepted · rejected.
- **Loan:** application · disclosures · processing · conditional approval · clear to close; **appraisal:** ordered · scheduled · received; **title:** search · commitment · clear to close · closing scheduled.

#### Notifications

Saved-home events, tour confirmations and changes, agreement sent and expiring, offer status and counter deadlines with countdown, every new task and document request, wire-fraud warnings at contract, CD and funding steps, closing countdown, homeowner digest, refi and anniversary/tax/PMI reminders. Per-type, per-channel settings that stay set, plus quiet hours (13.11; pain point from Zillow, Redfin and Realtor.com reviews).

#### Permissions

Each co-buyer has their own login, signature and document visibility on one shared deal (BP-X5). Guests get scoped access (BP-X6). Messages carry per-message visibility: all, agents only, private (BP-X1, Module 5). Uploads carry per-recipient share toggles (BP-R15, BP-C6). The buyer controls what activity the agent can see (consented analytics, BP-D42) and can switch agents with their data (BP-X7). Finance data is never resold (BP-X13).

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

In-portal copilot that answers listing, process and document questions with citations and hands off to a human (BP-X17); offer-strength estimator (BP-O2) shown as an estimate, not advice; plain-language offer and disclosure summaries (BP-O12) that the agent can review before release; AI Q&A about my home (BP-P14) allowed to say "refinancing doesn't make sense right now"; portal entry from ChatGPT or other assistants (BP-X16). AI never submits an offer, signs, or moves money.

#### Compliance hooks

Buyer agreement before touring and compensation clarity (US settlement, 7.9); RESPA lender-agnostic choice and affiliated-business disclosure (BP-R9; section 3); CFPB 3-business-day Closing Disclosure rule (BP-C29); FCRA/GLBA consent for loan data sharing (TXN.11); wire-fraud education and scam reporting; accessibility WCAG 2.2 AA; privacy center (download, delete, do-not-sell); regional packs: UK material information, UAE verified listings and DLD comps, India bank-loan marketplace and RERA ID, AU inspection planner and building reports (BP-X20).

#### Integrations

Lenders and POS/LOS (Blend, Encompass), Zillow Home Loans-style soft-pull partners, Down Payment Resource, Stripe Identity-style ID check, Earnnest-style EMD, Qualia/CertifID-style wire verification, Spectora/HomeGauge/Inspectify, Reggora, Matic/Covered insurance, HomeWiseDocs, Snapdocs/Proof RON, moving and utilities partners (no upsell, ECO-A5.2), Homebot-style home reports and AVM vendors.


### Module 3. Seller portal and listing management

**Rows:** 191 (92 Must, 74 Should, 24 Later, 1 context).

**Purpose.** One Listing record for the whole seller journey: valuation and CMA, net sheet, presentation, prep and media, MLS input and visibility rules, syndication, marketing, showings, feedback, open houses, offers and the hand-off to the transaction, with a branded seller portal on top. Today the agent works across Cloud CMA, Canva, ShowingTime, HomeLight Listing Management and Homebot (`features/02`). It also carries listing management for international agencies (UK, UAE, AU) and portal syndication with permit compliance.

**Users.** Listing agents, co-listers and assistants; showing coordinators and appointment desk staff; buyer agents (as requesting/offering agents, without an Estate OS seat); sellers and household members (seller portal); brokers (offer oversight, compliance); marketing staff.

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

#### 3.4 Listing prep: renovation, staging, media, disclosures (14 rows: 3 Must, 10 Should, 1 Later)

| ID | Feature | What it does | Priority | Benchmark leader | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SL-4.1 (+ECO-A6.1) | Pre-sale improvements paid at closing | Staging, flooring, painting and 100+ services fronted with nothing due until sale, termination or 12 months. Loans made by a licensed lender; affiliated-business disclosure required <br>**Also (ECO-A6.1, Pre-sale renovation, paid at closing):** Scoped improvements, a fixed-price bid and a project tracker, with the cost deducted on the settlement statement. <br>**Regulatory note:** Consumer credit and lending licensing (Compass uses a licensed lender). Agent compensation from a contractor or lender is a RESPA risk if a federally related loan is involved. | Should (from ECO-A6.1; SL-4.1 was Later (partner)) | Compass Concierge· Compass Concierge: "zero due until closing". Concierge Capital loans come through Notable Finance (NMLS 1824748) (compass.com; agent PDF). A Compass agent page lists $25k for a flat fee, or up to $75k at **9.99% interest + 2.99% origination** (brianhuie.com). Curbio: fixed-price proposal "within 48 hours", pay-at-close through financing partners, one-year warranty, live project app (curbio.com) | [compass.com](https://www.compass.com/concierge/) ; [agent PDF](https://www.bethgassel.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/compass-concierge.pdf) ; [brianhuie.com](https://www.brianhuie.com/applytoconcierge) ; [curbio.com](https://curbio.com/how-it-works) ; https://www.compass.com/concierge/ |
| SL-4.2 | Managed pre-list renovation marketplace | Fixed-price contracts; project manager; instant estimate in an app at the kitchen table; inspection report turned into repair pricing in 24h; real-time project updates | Should (integration) | Curbio | https://curbio.com/ |
| SL-4.3 | Prep phases tied to staged launch | Private exclusive while work is under way, then coming soon, then MLS (see §6 rules) | Should | Compass Concierge flow | https://www.compass.com/concierge/ |
| SL-4.4 | Media ordering with photographer marketplace | Order from preferred or approved local photographers; one login across media, transaction and showing tools | Should | Zillow Showcase update | https://grow.zillow.com/agents/updated-showcase-experience-has-arrived |
| SL-4.5 | Media delivery hub | Full-resolution plus automatic MLS-sized copies; video; 3D (Matterport, iGuide, Zillow 3D Home) as branded or unbranded; floor plans synced from CubiCasa; scheduled delivery email | Must | Aryeo on zillow.help | https://www.zillow.help/article/uploading-and-delivering-media ; https://www.aryeo.com/ |
| SL-4.6 | Interactive floor plan linked to photos | Rooms on the floor plan link to their photos. Zillow reports 79% more saves for listings with one | Should | ShowingTime+ Showcase launch | https://showingtime.com/resources/blog/new-immersive-listing-experience |
| SL-4.7 | 3D digital twin with tags and measurement | Dollhouse view, walkthrough, tags (appliances, amenities), measure tool, privacy blur, floor plans as PNG, PDF or SVG in 2 business days | Should (integration) | Matterport | https://matterport.com/digital-twin-features ; https://matterport.com/learn/digital-twin/real-estate |
| SL-4.8 | Human photo editing and virtual staging service | 24h edits and 48h virtual staging, item removal, day-to-dusk, floor-plan redraw, renders; pay as you go with free reworks | Should (integration) | BoxBrownie | https://www.boxbrownie.com/ |
| SL-4.9 | AI virtual staging with an original-plus-staged pair | Export the staged image together with the original so either can be published, as some MLSs and California require (see §5) | Should | StageMLS (vendor); Aryeo AI staging | https://stagemls.com/mls-virtual-staging-rules ; https://www.aryeo.com/ |
| SL-4.10 | Disclosure package with tracked access | Fill seller forms, e-sign (DocuSign), share one secure link. Tracks each view, download and return visit by each buyer party | Must | HLM | https://www.homelight.com/hlm |
| SL-4.11 | AI disclosure summary cited to the page | Summarises the disclosure package and cites the page for each point, so the agent can answer buyer questions | Should | HLM | https://www.homelight.com/hlm |
| SL-4.12 | Buyer engagement scoring | Ranks buyer parties by how closely they are reading (for example 37 of 37 documents, 9 visits) to predict who will write an offer | Should | HLM | https://www.homelight.com/hlm |
| SL-4.13 | Documents auto-sent with showing requests | Attach disclosures or HOA documents (PDF, JPG, GIF, PNG, 20 MB max) that email automatically to agents who request a showing | Must | ShowingTime Listing Setup | https://showingtime.uservoice.com/knowledgebase/articles/2005555-listing-setup-tab |
| SL-4.14 | On-demand helpers for prep tasks | Licensed agents for phone photography and condition reports, lockbox and sign placement | Later | Showami | https://www.showami.com/ |

#### 3.5 Listing input, MLS compliance and visibility rules (19 rows: 14 Must, 3 Should, 2 Later)

| ID | Feature | What it does | Priority | Benchmark leader | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SL-5.1 (+PC-7.4) | Marketing-option chooser with seller certification | Full MLS, office exclusive (filed but not disseminated) or delayed marketing (IDX and syndication held for the MLS-set period). Each exempt option needs a seller-signed disclosure acknowledging the benefits waived <br>**Also (PC-7.4, Exempt-listing states (Office Exclusive, Delayed Marketing) with seller-disclosure capture):** The listing object holds exempt type, MLS-set delay period, signed seller disclosure and timers. The system blocks IDX and syndication until the delay ends | Must | NAR Multiple Listing Options for Sellers· NAR *Multiple Listing Options for Sellers*, effective 25 Mar 2025, implemented by 30 Sep 2025 (NAR) | [NAR](https://www.nar.realtor/about-nar/policies/multiple-listing-options-for-sellers) ; https://www.nar.realtor/about-nar/policies/multiple-listing-options-for-sellers |
| SL-5.2 (+PC-7.5) | Clear Cooperation 1-business-day clock | Any public marketing (window flyer, yard sign, public site, IDX/VOW, email blast, multi-brokerage network, public app) starts a 1-business-day deadline to submit to the MLS. Opting out is not allowed <br>**Also (PC-7.5, Clear Cooperation timer and "public marketing" detector):** Flags multi-brokerage or public marketing of an exempt listing, which triggers the one-business-day MLS submission | Must | NAR CCP; Northstar FAQ· NAR: one-to-one broker-to-broker communications don't trigger CCP; multi-brokerage communications do (NAR); OneKey MLS summary (search snippet) | [NAR](https://www.nar.realtor/about-nar/policies/multiple-listing-options-for-sellers) ; https://www.nar.realtor/about-nar/policies/mls-clear-cooperation-policy ; https://resources.northstarmls.com/hc/en-us/articles/54550198335771-Clear-Cooperation-Policy |
| SL-5.3 | "Public marketing" event log | Every flyer, post, sign order, email blast or website publish for the property is logged with a timestamp and checked against the clock. One-to-one broker messages are exempt; multi-brokerage messages count as public | Must | NAR MLOS (1:1 vs multi-brokerage) | https://www.nar.realtor/about-nar/policies/multiple-listing-options-for-sellers |
| SL-5.4 (+PC-7.6) | Zillow Listing Access Standards guardrail | Zillow's 2026 standard requires listings to be "broadly accessible"; registration-walled or gated pre-marketing is not shown on Zillow. The original 2025 version required MLS entry within 1 business day; later reports describe warnings and blocks <br>**Also (PC-7.6, Portal-policy guard (Zillow Listing Access Standards)):** Warns before an action would make a listing "publicly marketed but not broadly accessible", the condition that triggers Zillow bans | Must | RealEstateNews Mar 2026; strike detail (secondary)· Zillow LAS updated 17 Mar 2026: "If listings are publicly marketed, they should be broadly accessible" (Zillow); 48 bans and 1,202 violations by Nov 2025 (Mike DelPrete); Compass "See It First" and the 15 Sep 2026 ruling (Real Estate News) | [Zillow](https://www.zillow.com/news/updating-zillows-listing-access-standards-for-todays-market/) ; [Mike DelPrete](https://www.mikedp.com/articles/2025/11/25/zillows-listing-ban-and-violation-metrics) ; [Real Estate News](https://www.realestatenews.com/2026/09/22/the-future-of-zillow-and-compass-as-see-it-first-goes-live) ; https://www.realestatenews.com/2026/03/19/zillows-simplified-listing-standards-make-the-mls-optional ; https://www.open-homes.com/blog/zillows-listing-access-standards-what-every-agent-should-learn-from-the-48-banned-listings/ |
| SL-5.5 | Safe-teaser checker | Blocks a "coming soon" social post that contains address, price or a call to action before the listing is live | Should | Open-homes summary of Zillow guidance (secondary) | https://www.open-homes.com/blog/zillows-listing-access-standards-what-every-agent-should-learn-from-the-48-banned-listings/ |
| SL-5.6 | Delayed-marketing DOM and price-history handling | Store the MLS-specific DOM and price-history rules that apply during a delay | Must | NAR MLOS FAQ items 9–11 | https://www.nar.realtor/about-nar/policies/multiple-listing-options-for-sellers |
| SL-5.7 | Seller internet opt-outs | Seller can withhold the listing or just the address from internet display; signed opt-out form uploaded to the MLS and kept at least 1 year | Must | OneKey | https://support.onekeymls.com/hc/en-us/articles/43309998351124-Seller-Opt-Out-Provisions |
| SL-5.8 | AVM and comments switch-off on IDX | At the seller's request, AVMs and third-party comments next to the listing must be disabled on all participants' IDX sites | Must | CVR MLS 11.2.8 (NAR model rule) | https://resourcecenter.cvrmls.com/article-11-internet-data-exchange-idx/section-11-2-8-comments-and-automated-valuation-models-avm/ |
| SL-5.9 | Photo rule linter | Primary photo must be the front exterior and unaltered; deadlines (48h or 2 business days, with escalating fines); no branding, watermarks, agent photos, QR codes, people or signs | Must | MLS Now; NorthstarMLS | https://support.mlsnow.com/hc/en-us/articles/24915189546907-Listing-Photo-Rules-and-Requirements ; https://resources.northstarmls.com/hc/en-us/articles/54550054122267-MLS-Photo-Rules |
| SL-5.10 | Digital-alteration disclosure engine | Per-MLS rules: Stellar remarks text plus a ban on overlays; CRMLS original placed next to the altered photo; ARMLS "Digitally Altered" stamp ($200 fines from Dec 2026). California AB 723 (since Jan 1 2026) requires a disclosure plus a link or QR code to the original at a public URL. Structural changes and hidden defects are banned | Must | StageMLS rule table (vendor, Aug 2026); MLS Now Rule 3.3 | https://stagemls.com/mls-virtual-staging-rules ; https://support.mlsnow.com/hc/en-us/articles/24915189546907-Listing-Photo-Rules-and-Requirements |
| SL-5.11 | No compensation in MLS fields | Strip compensation from MLS-bound data and history; keep it on the deal record and the seller's net sheet only | Must | OneKey | https://support.onekeymls.com/hc/en-us/articles/28842130579988-NAR-Settlement-Compensation-Fields-to-be-Removed-from-the-MLS |
| SL-5.12 | Fair-housing copy and image check | Flags wording, photos or symbols that signal a preference by race, colour, religion, sex, handicap, familial status or national origin, across ads, flyers, signs and remarks | Must | 24 CFR 100.75; checker tools exist, for example | https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/24/100.75 ; https://www.quicklistingai.com/tools/listing-compliance-checker |

| SL-5.15 | Co-lister and team access mapping | Rechat cannot give listing dashboards to team or co-listers unless the MLS assigns them. Estate OS should map them explicitly | Should | Rechat availability notes | https://help.rechat.com/guides/marketing/listing-marketing |

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B15 | **Concessions vs price confusion inflates commission** | Sellers | "negotiating 'seller concessions' still means you are paying the realtors on the full sale price… realtors got an extra $1125" ([Trustpilot Redfin](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/redfin.com)) | Seller net sheet comparing "price cut" with "concession" side by side, including commission impact. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Win the listing.** Seller requests a valuation online (or the agent sees a CMA request intent signal) → agent builds the CMA from MLS comps (radius or polygon, adjustments, off-MLS comps) on top of an AVM starting point → suggested price range page → title-accurate net sheet with concession and buyer-broker lines → listing presentation from brokerage-locked templates, presented live or sent as print/digital/flipbook → presentation views are tracked on the contact.
2. **Prepare and take it live.** Prep plan (pay-at-close renovation or partner estimate) → media order and delivery hub (MLS-sized copies, 3D, floor plans) → disclosure package with tracked access → seller chooses marketing option (full MLS, office exclusive, delayed marketing) and signs the certification → photo-rule and digital-alteration linter, fair-housing and truthfulness checks on copy → staged launch (private network → coming soon → MLS) with the distribution matrix showing where the listing is visible and why → syndication to chosen portals (blocked automatically while exempt or delayed, or without a Dubai permit).
3. **Market it.** MLS status change drafts the lifecycle kit (Just Listed / Open House / New Price / Under Contract / Sold): property website, social posts adapted per channel, flyers and window cards with tracked QR, agent e-blast, Meta ad in the housing Special Ad Category → every public action is written to the public-marketing log that drives the Clear Cooperation clock → results flow into the seller marketing report.
4. **Show it.** Agent sets appointment type, availability rules, lead time, buffers and occupant privacy → buyer agents request (with a buyer-agreement acknowledgement before instructions are released) → seller-occupied listings route approval to the seller by SMS one-tap → time-boxed access codes appear in the app only inside the window, with geofenced arrival push and safety timer → "showing complete" alert to the seller → automatic feedback request → agent publishes or holds feedback for the seller → feedback sentiment, showing counts and portal views roll into a pricing-health card.
5. **Open house.** Sign-in page auto-created from the MLS schedule → kiosk or QR sign-in, offline, required fields, "working with an agent?" → instant text with the listing link → attendees added to the CRM with a follow-up plan → seller-ready open-house report.
6. **Offers to accepted contract.** Agent publishes offer instructions (deadline, bully policy, required documents) and an offer link in MLS remarks → each buyer agent gets a private offer room; read receipts; new-offer alerts on every channel → terms extracted into fields; completeness and risk check → side-by-side comparison with net-to-seller per offer, shared read-only with the seller → mass "highest and best" → counter, accept or decline in one place; written offer-presented affirmation on request (NAR SoP 1-6/1-7) → on acceptance the public link closes, the listing converts to the transaction file with dates (7.1) and the seller portal switches to milestones.

#### States and statuses

- **Listing:** pre-listing · private exclusive · coming soon · active (MLS) · office exclusive · delayed marketing (with `delay_until`) · under contract / pending · sold · expired · withdrawn · cancelled. Date-driven expiry (TX-1.3).
- **Distribution per channel:** off · scheduled · live · paused · blocked (reason: exempt, LAS risk, missing permit, portal rejected).
- **Showing:** requested · confirmed · declined · rescheduled · cancelled · completed · no-show (silent edits allowed, SL-8.18).
- **Feedback:** requested · received · held for review · published to seller.
- **Offer:** registered · received · viewed · countered · accepted · declined · backup · withdrawn · expired.
- **Clear Cooperation clock:** not started · running (first public marketing event) · submitted to MLS · breached.

#### Notifications

To the agent: showing requests, confirmations, cancellations, arrivals, completions, feedback received, new offers, offer deadlines, CCP clock warnings, missing-photo deadlines, permit expiry, portal rejections, disclosure downloads. To the seller: showing requests to approve, showing complete, published feedback, marketing activity, new offers (if shared), price or status change, milestones. To buyer agents: read receipts, offer status, back-on-market and price-reduced alerts to past showers.

#### Permissions

Owner and occupant contact details never reach requesting agents; buyer-agent identity hidden on seller reports by default (SL-9.4). Seller portal shows only items the agent marks visible (SL-1.3). Seller offer room is read-only and cannot message buyer agents. Co-listers and team members are mapped explicitly, not only via MLS assignment (SL-5.15). Posting another agent's listing requires permission (SL-5.14). Tablet presentation mode hides commission and owner data (06 UX pattern).

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

AI CMA picks comps and drafts the narrative for agent edit (SL-2.6); AI listing copy with a fair-housing and truthfulness pass before publish (SL-7.20); AI listing video in the background with credit refund on failure (SL-7.9); AI disclosure summary cited to the page (SL-4.11); buyer engagement scoring (SL-4.12); AI feedback sentiment (SL-9.6); AI deal summary (SL-12.9); AI virtual staging always exported with the original (SL-4.9). Nothing publishes to the MLS, a portal or social without agent approval; the safe-teaser checker blocks non-compliant posts.

#### Compliance hooks

NAR Clear Cooperation and Multiple Listing Options for Sellers; Zillow Listing Access Standards; MLS photo rules and digital-alteration disclosure (incl. California AB 723); seller opt-outs and IDX AVM/comment switch-off; no compensation in MLS fields; fair housing (24 CFR 100.75) on copy, images and ad targeting; Meta housing Special Ad Category; NAR Code of Ethics Article 12 and SoP 1-6/1-7; Ontario TRESA seller-directed offer disclosure; UK material information Parts A–C; Victoria Property Price Statement; Dubai Trakheesi permit, DED number and Madmoun QR; listing-data reuse permissions. Each check writes to the audit trail (section 3).

#### Integrations

MLS input and status (RESO), ShowingTime/BrokerBay/Aligned patterns, Master Lock and Supra lockboxes, Aryeo/Matterport/BoxBrownie/CubiCasa, Curbio/Compass Concierge-style pay-at-close renovation, Canva round-trip, Meta and Google ads, title-company fee schedules for net sheets, Opendoor/HomeLight cash-offer partners, UK portals (Rightmove, Zoopla, OnTheMarket), UAE portals (Property Finder, Bayut, dubizzle), AU real-time portal sync, Zillow Showcase/Preview where allowed.


### Module 4. CRM and lead routing

**Rows:** 120 (75 Must, 41 Should, 4 Later).

**Purpose.** The agent and team front office: capture leads from every source, route them in seconds with an explainable rule engine, keep one record per person and household, run smart lists, action plans and automations, and hold agents accountable. The bar is Follow Up Boss, Lofty, BoldTrail, Sierra Interactive and Real Geeks (`features/03`), plus Sell.Do-grade routing for developers and Reapit/Goyzer-grade applicant matching for international agencies (`features/06`).

**Users.** Agents, ISAs, team leads, admins and brokers; lenders and partners as collaborators; developer pre-sales reps; recruiting staff (recruits are Persons with a candidate role, Module 8).

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

| CRM-3.6 | Speed-to-lead metrics | Average speed to action, first call, first text and first email, counting only 1:1 messages from the assigned agent. | CRM-3.10 | Response-window benchmarks per source | In-product guidance by source type. #### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B5 | **Four or more tools for basic tasks; can't prospect owners** | Agents | Forrester for TransUnion: "48% of agents rely on four or more separate tools"; "47%… prospecting gap… compliance rules that prohibit them from obtaining contact information" ([TransUnion](https://www.transunion.com/blog/fragmentation-real-estate-tech)) | Owner and contact enrichment inside the CRM, with DNC/TCPA consent tracking on every number. |
| B9 | **All-or-nothing suite; agents take leads elsewhere** | Agents | "It very all-or-nothing now." "I get my leads out of kvCORE & take those elsewhere." "Ensure leads sent to agents aren't only after 2am" (same source) | Modular licensing; lead routing with quiet hours and fallback. |
| B11 | **Consumers spammed after one valuation request** | Sellers/buyers | "14 calls (and counting) from real estate brokers in less than 10 hours" ([Trustpilot HomeLight](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/homelight.com)); "gave my phone number away to hundreds of salesman" ([Trustpilot Realtor.com](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/realtor.com)) | Consent-scoped lead capture: the consumer picks one agent; no resale of contacts; one-click "stop contacting me". |

#### Key workflows

1. **Lead in.** A lead arrives as a typed event (Registration, Inquiry, Seller Inquiry, Property Inquiry, Viewed Property, Saved Property, Visited Open House, Incoming Call…) from email parse, API, pixel, portal connector, Facebook/Google lead ads, Indian or UAE portals, walk-in kiosk, missed-call number, sign-rider text code or unknown caller → dedupe against existing Persons (locked source of truth, CP credit per policy) → optional "wait for phone" delay → source registry applies its own routing, lender and automation.
2. **Routing.** Rules evaluate top to bottom (scope company/office/team/personal by lead owner; conditions on source, tag, lead type, geography, price, MLS number, phone; ALL/ANY logic; working hours) → distribution: weighted round robin ("hunger"), first-to-claim by push or simultaneous call, claim with timeout, geographic round robin, direct, or pond → undeletable catch-all → on-assign actions (tag, plan, alerts, auto-text from the right number, AI ISA opt-in, lender) → routing log shows Y/N per rule; "simulate this lead" preview.
3. **Speed to lead.** Initial auto-text within the configured delay, from the assigned agent's number → "new lead call" rings the agent and bridges on keypress, retrying up to 9 times over 3 days → unworked inquiries escalate to an admin → idle-lead auto-reassignment if no manual contact within the window (automated messages do not count) → all inside the quiet-hours actionable window.
4. **Nurture.** Lead joins smart lists by filter (default lists teach the method: "Qualify: Needs Contact") → action plan or automation (trigger → conditions → delays → actions incl. AI steps) → auto-pause on reply or a call longer than 2.5 minutes → behavioral auto-messages on repeat visits and views → date-based yearly automations (birthday, purchase anniversary).
5. **Deal.** Stage moves (manually, or AI-proposed with "ask me first") → buyer or seller deal pipeline with forecast → buyer agreement status shown natively on the Person and Deal (7.9) → offer tracked → closing creates the homeowner record (2.6) and the deal source feeds source ROI (12.1).
6. **Accountability.** Agent fails login or task-completion thresholds → removed from rotation until next month with emails to agent and admin → manager reinstates manually; pond claims and approvals logged for coaching.

#### States and statuses

- **Lead stage:** exactly one per lead, account-defined (e.g. Lead → Attempted contact → Spoke with customer → Appointment set → Met → Under contract → Closed → Past client / Sphere / Nurture / Trash). Segments (static or active) and tags are many per lead (CRM-4.8).
- **Assignment:** unassigned · offered (claim timer running) · claimed/assigned · in pond · reassigned · idle (timer expired).
- **Agent availability:** available · paused · removed from rotation (accountability) · roster-only / inactive / hidden.
- **Task:** today · overdue · future · done; auto-generated campaign tasks auto-delete 7 days after overdue (CRM-7.2).
- **Appointment:** created · upcoming · held with outcome · no-show · cancelled.
- **Deal:** pipeline stage per pipeline (buyer, seller, developer booking), plus forecast probability.

#### Notifications

New-lead alerts by email, SMS, push and in-app (WhatsApp for agencies that prefer it); first-to-claim push ("tap claims it, swipe loses it", with a test-notification tool); priority alerts tiered by intent (text only for raised hands, AI hand-offs and voice transfers); opportunity notifications (high interest, back to site, sell intent, back on market); task due; appointment reminders to the consumer; pond activity to the Pond Lead; accountability notices.

#### Permissions

Ownership scope company → office → team → agent decides routing and visibility; personally sourced leads can be hidden from admins ("Private Lead #ID", CRM-2.20); collaborators see the lead, non-collaborator assignees see only their task or thread; assistants can update notes and tasks but not export or see billing; pond governance limits leads per agent and channels allowed; exports notify the owner (13.4). Ownership stamp decides what an agent takes on departure (13.5).

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

Explainable lead score with top indicators (CRM-15.3) and tiered intent score; AI lead-health analysis after import; AI steps inside drips (classifier, generator, summarizer, extractor) with branch-on-result; AI-moved pipeline stage with Automatic / Ask me first / Off; natural-language filter builder; AI ISA enrollment from a routing rule (Module 6). AI never reassigns a lead or changes stage in "ask me first" mode without approval; every AI-sent message passes the consent gate (5.4).

#### Compliance hooks

TCPA consent and revocation, DNC scrubbing, quiet hours by the lead's time zone, 10DLC (5.4); buyer-agreement tracking native on the record (US settlement); Canada CASL and Texas TREC notices; DPDP consent for India; RESPA on lender co-assignment (no pay-per-referral); fair-housing check on AI-written messages.

#### Integrations

Zillow two-way and Tech Connect, Realtor.com/Opcity, Facebook and Google lead ads, 99acres, MagicBricks, Housing and 16+ Indian portals, Property Finder, Bayut and dubizzle, IDX/website providers, Gmail/Outlook and calendars, Zapier, MCP, DNC.com, enrichment providers; Ylopo/Structurely-style AI vendors depend on stable ponds, tags and inbox numbers (CRM-20.10).


### Module 5. Unified inbox and communications

**Rows:** 58 (46 Must, 12 Should, 0 Later).

**Purpose.** One inbox and one conversation history for every person and every deal, lease and booking: SMS/MMS, RCS, WhatsApp, Instagram and Messenger DMs, email, calls, voicemail, video messages, portal messages and partner inbox apps, auto-logged and assignable, with consent enforced before every send. It must beat FUB's inbox (which leaves WhatsApp "being considered" and cannot call outside US/Canada), match Hostaway/Guesty's STR inbox and Sell.Do's WhatsApp/telephony hub, and fix the "bypass the CRM" data loss (`01-market-research.md`).

**Users.** Agents, ISAs, TCs, property managers, leasing and maintenance staff, developer pre-sales and CRM teams, CP managers; consumers on the other end (buyers, sellers, tenants, owners, vendors, guests, CPs), who reply from their own channel.

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B13 | **Messages from portals break; support takes a week** | Agents | "Accessing client messages for active listings is completely broken… waiting an entire week just to get a call back" ([Trustpilot Zillow](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/zillow.com)) | Unified inbox that ingests portal emails, SMS and calls even if the portal app fails. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Inbound message.** Message arrives on any channel → matched to a Person by number/email/handle (unknown numbers create a lead, 4.1) → attached to the right object (deal, listing, lease, work order, booking) → lands in the personal or team inbox by routing and office hours (after-hours inbox) → assigned → reply from any channel; the thread stays one thread (channel bridging, BP-X2) → closed conversations reopen when the person replies.
2. **Deal thread.** Buyer, co-buyer, agent, TC, lender and title share one thread per deal with per-message visibility (all / agents only / private) → anyone can answer by SMS, WhatsApp or email without installing the app → wiring instructions are never sent in the thread; the system flags any message that contains them (13.13).
3. **Outbound send (human, automation, AI or API).** Compose → send pipeline checks ConsentRecord for that number and channel, suppression list (opt-out, DNC, bounce), quiet hours in the recipient's time zone, frequency caps, 10DLC registration, DLT template (India) and WhatsApp template approval → send from the right number (personal, team, role or masked) → opt-out footer appended where required → delivery, read and click events land on the timeline.
4. **Call.** Click-to-call (VoIP or bridge to the agent's mobile) or mobile dialer → state-aware recording disclosure → call logged with recording, transcript and AI summary → disposition → follow-up task or text. Power dialer and call lists run from smart lists, on mobile too.
5. **Opt-out.** STOP/UNSUBSCRIBE keywords or free-text revocation ("stop texting me", detected by AI) → revocation recorded with timestamp and channel → applied across all numbers and message types → internal DNC updated.

#### States and statuses

- **Conversation:** open · assigned · snoozed · closed (reopens on reply) · spam/reported.
- **Message delivery:** queued (held by quiet hours) · sent · delivered · read · failed · blocked (no consent / DNC / unregistered).
- **Consent per number and channel:** System Recorded Consent · User Recorded Consent · Business Relationship · Unknown · Internal DNC; revoked (with source).
- **10DLC brand/campaign:** draft · submitted · approved · rejected (with reason code).
- **Call:** ringing · answered · missed · voicemail · transferred · recorded.

#### Notifications

New message and missed call (push deep-links to the exact thread, never a blank screen, 13.11); assignment; SLA breach on unanswered threads; AI takeover queue items (AI_NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP); negative-sentiment escalation; delivery failures and consent blocks; template performance changes.

#### Permissions

Inbox membership per team inbox; conversation assignment shows a non-collaborator only that thread; per-message visibility on deal threads; recordings downloadable only by roles with that grant; masked calling hides consumer numbers from reps and CPs (DEV-3.15); WhatsApp/telephony costs are pass-through meters visible to the payer (13.9).

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

Call transcripts and summaries; suggested replies and AI writing (tone, length, translation into 30–60+ languages); AI revocation detection; negative-sentiment escalation; AI conversation takeover queue for threads run by the AI ISA. AI drafts are approved by the human unless the admin has set that message type to auto-send; AI always identifies itself.

#### Compliance hooks

TCPA prior express consent and revocation "in any reasonable manner"; national, state and litigator DNC (now covering texts); CTIA STOP/START/HELP; A2P 10DLC; carrier spam-label protection; state all-party call-recording consent; CAN-SPAM (physical address, opt-out); Canada CASL; India DLT, NDNC and DPDP consent; GDPR lawful basis for UK/EU; Meta WhatsApp template rules. See section 3.

#### Integrations

Twilio-class SMS/voice, carrier registration (TCR), WhatsApp Business API via 20+ BSPs, Instagram/Messenger, RCS, Gmail/Microsoft 365 two-way sync, SendGrid-class bulk email, 25+ Indian telephony providers, RingCentral/Zoom/Teams caller insights, Airbnb/Vrbo/Booking messaging (via 9.23), DNC.com, CallAction Verify, BombBomb-style video, Zillow/RealScout/CallAction inbox apps.


### Module 6. AI agents layer

**Rows:** 47 (14 Must, 26 Should, 7 Later).

**Purpose.** The shared AI layer: AI ISAs that text and call leads, copilots and assistants for every role, a workflow engine where AI agents run steps under supervision, and the governance that makes AI safe to sell to brokers, regulators and residents. Module-specific AI (contract reading in 7, maintenance triage in 9, launch forecasting in 10, CMA in 3) runs on this layer and follows its rules. The bar: Ylopo, Lofty Sales Agent and Cowork, Rechat Lucy, AppFolio Realm-X, Entrata ELI+, Hostaway AI, Sell.Do Jarvis. The anti-bar: DoorLoop and FolioSpace residents forced into an AI chat (`features/05`, `features/08` B27).

**Users.** Agents, ISAs and team leads (AI ISA supervision); every staff role (copilot); admins (autonomy policy, knowledge base, budgets); consumers (who talk to AI agents and must always be able to reach a person).

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.
