#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B22 | **No human support in emergencies; AI chat wall** | PMs, tenants | "someone hack our log ins and we cant get emergency assistance. We stuck in AI chat" (same source); "Customer support is horrendous. It can take weeks to get a live person" ([TrustRadius AppFolio](https://www.trustradius.com/products/appfolio-property-manager/reviews)) | Human escalation SLA for account lockout and payments; the AI assistant always offers "talk to a person". |

#### Key workflows

1. **AI ISA conversation.** Lead enrolled (routing rule toggle, "Start conversation" button or automation step; eligibility rules exclude DNC, landlines, CSV imports without consent) → consent check → AI identity disclosure in the first message, opt-out footer → proactive, behavioral and responsive modes, at most one message per channel per day unless the lead replies, inside contact hours → qualification → appointment booked on the agent's calendar or live voice transfer to the first agent who answers → dispositions written as tags (continue, stop, pause until next priority event) → hand-off puts the thread in the takeover queue with a summary.
2. **Copilot action.** User asks in chat ("find yesterday's site visitors not contacted and draft intro texts") → copilot answers for free in chat mode, or proposes actions in "do" mode → user reviews the batch (approve, edit, dismiss, always approve this type) → actions run with the user's permissions, metered as AI credits, logged to the ledger.
3. **Workflow with embedded agents.** Admin builds a flow (trigger on event, relative date or schedule → steps → conditions) from templates (applications, collections, renewals, unit turns, listing) → an AI performer runs a step (pre-qualify, schedule, intake) → interrupt logic pauses the flow when a person replies or a condition hits and passes full context to staff → status board shows in-progress and behind-schedule flows; a step can be reassigned in two clicks.
4. **Daily brief.** Each morning the user gets one SMS or in-app brief: yesterday's AI activity, deadlines at risk, and one recommended lead or action with the reason; skipped if nothing happened.
5. **Governance setup.** Admin sets autonomy per agent and per action, model region and training opt-out, PII redaction, monthly AI spend cap, knowledge base per property/community/team, and required attestations (e.g. fair-housing training before AI ads, BO.8).

#### States and statuses

- **AI action:** proposed · approved · auto-approved (policy) · executed · dismissed · failed · reverted.
- **Autonomy level per action type:** suggest · draft · act with approval · autonomous.
- **AI ISA enrollment:** eligible · active · engaged · needs human (AI_NEEDS_FOLLOW_UP) · transferred · stopped (opt-out, DNC, not interested) · paused until next priority event.
- **Flow run:** running · waiting · interrupted (human hand-off) · behind schedule · completed · failed.

#### Notifications

AI hand-off and live transfer, takeover-queue items with target response time (2–4 hours), approval requests ("Needs-you" list), daily brief, spend-cap thresholds, failed or reverted actions, negative-sentiment escalations.

#### Permissions

AI, MCP and API actions are capped by the granting user's permissions (PC-2.10). Who can run each AI capability is a separate grant (e.g. AI Create/AI Update on contracts, TX-7.6). Enabling AI agents is a sensitive permission (PC-2.5). Consumers can always reach a human: every AI chat and voice line offers "talk to a person" (pain point B22).

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop)

This module *is* the human-in-the-loop policy: autonomy levels with conservative defaults, an AI action ledger with prompt, tool calls, model, user and approver, AI identity disclosure, consent check before outreach, fair-housing guardrails on generated copy, ad targeting and search, high-risk AI impact assessments for screening, pricing and lead scoring (Colorado SB24-205), model and prompt versioning with evals, and data-boundary controls. Pricing for AI ISA follows enrolled leads or database size, not seats (CRM-13.12 pricing benchmarks).

#### Compliance hooks

TCPA (AI voice is an "artificial voice"; prior express consent), FCC revocation, fair housing (24 CFR 100.75 and HUD 2024 AI advertising guidance, not verified), Colorado AI Act (effective 30 Jun 2026), FCRA adverse action when AI informs screening (9.4), DPDP and GDPR for data sent to model providers, AI data-handling disclosure for MCP (TX-16.9).

#### Integrations

LLM providers with regional routing, telephony and messaging (Module 5), calendar, MCP clients (Claude, ChatGPT, Gemini), Teams/Slack for agents that live in chat tools, Structurely/21X-class AI call centres as build-vs-partner options.


### Module 7. Transactions, compliance and closing

**Rows:** 170 (107 Must, 55 Should, 7 Later, 1 context).

**Purpose.** One file per deal from buyer agreement or listing to closing: forms, documents, e-signature, checklists, broker compliance review, contract-derived timelines with AI, TC workflows, MLS status sync and the closing services around the deal (mortgage, title, insurance, inspection, appraisal, notary, 1031). It must match SkySlope, Paperless Pipeline, ListedKit, dotloop, Lone Wolf and Rechat Deals, and it hands money to the back office (Module 8) on the same record, which is the gap between SkySlope Suite and SkySlope Books today (`features/04`).

**Users.** Agents, co-agents, TCs (in-house or outside), office admins, auditors, brokers of record; buyers and sellers (signing, uploads via portals); lenders, title/escrow officers, inspectors and attorneys as external parties.

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

#### 7.1 Transaction file and lifecycle (25 rows: 17 Must, 7 Should, 1 Later)

| ID | Feature | What it does | Priority | Benchmark leader | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Each status can hold its own company-named, colored stages (e.g. Inspection, Underwriting, Clear to Close). A status change drops the deal into the first stage of the new status. | TX-1.6 | Status set per transaction type (as in dotloop's API) | Each transaction type has its own status set:<br>• **PURCHASE_OFFER:** PRE_OFFER → UNDER_CONTRACT → SOLD → ARCHIVED<br>• **LISTING_FOR_SALE:** PRE_LISTING → PRIVATE_LISTING → ACTIVE_LISTING → UNDER_CONTRACT → SOLD → ARCHIVED<br>• **LISTING_FOR_LEASE** and **LEASE_OFFER:** end in LEASED<br>• **OTHER:** NEW → IN_PROGRESS → DONE | Must | DL Public API v2 §17.1.3 | [Public API v2 §17.1.3](https://dotloop.github.io/public-api/) |
| TX-1.7 | Double-ended (dual) files | **Option 1:** one shared file with both a listing and a transaction checklist, and a co-agent. **Option 2:** two linked files, one per agent, so each side's commission is calculated separately. | Must | SS Double-Ended; SSB Dual Deals | [Double-Ended](https://support.skyslope.com/support/solutions/articles/156000366451-creating-double-ended-transactions) ; [Dual Deals](https://bookssupport.skyslope.com/support/solutions/articles/158000121998-dual-deals) |
| TX-1.8 | Referral-only file type | Tracks outgoing referrals for compliance and commission, even with no property. SS's workaround is a filler address such as "01 Referral". REAL treats a referral as a normal transaction, subject to splits and fees. | Must | SS Submit a Referral; REAL Referral splits | [Submit a Referral](https://support.skyslope.com/support/solutions/articles/156000366455-how-to-submit-a-referral-in-skyslope) ; [Referral splits](https://support.therealbrokerage.com/hc/en-us/articles/27728332605975-Are-Referral-Transactions-Subject-to-Splits-and-Fees) |
| TX-1.9 | Lease and rental transactions | Lease listing and lease transaction types, with tenant screening started from inside the deal: LW via RentSpree (June 2026), BTBO via Rental Beast (July 2026, paid by the applicant). | Should | LW June 2026; BTBO Release Notes | [June 2026](https://www.lwolf.com/resources/june-2026-release-notes) ; [Release Notes](http://support.brokermint.com/en/articles/9140375-release-notes) |

RPR: 52% "not confident or only somewhat confident" using AI content with clients ([RealEstateNews](https://www.realestatenews.com/2026/02/16/agents-want-more-and-better-ai-tools-and-more-training)) | Contract-to-timeline extraction, auto client status updates and disclosure summaries, each with cited source clauses and an approve step. |
| B16 | **Buyer agreements still confusing two years on** | Buyers, agents | "LernMore says consumers still struggle with key clauses"; risk "whether clients can later claim they did not grasp key terms" ([HousingWire 2026-08-18](https://www.housingwire.com/articles/buyer-agreements-lernmore-confusion/)) | Buyer agreement with clause-by-clause explainer, multi-language support, and a comprehension acknowledgement logged before the first tour. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Open a file.** From a Buyer Record (buyer agreement on file), an accepted offer on a listing (3.11), an MLS number, a dropped contract (AI Create), an agent intake form, an email to the intake inbox, or a UAE deal wizard → representation type (purchase, listing, both, lease tenant/landlord, referral, BPO, other) sets required fields, checklist and commission logic → AI extracts property, parties, dates and commission with a citation on every field → nothing is final until reviewed.
2. **Timeline.** Execution date is the root → every deadline computed (calendar or business days, holidays, "calendar day landing on a business day") → tasks anchored to dates or to other tasks; overrides flagged and skipped in later cascades → amendments trigger AI Update → key dates sync to calendars with invitations → conditional mini-templates added when a scenario occurs (inspection delayed, short sale, probate, HOA).
3. **Documents and signing.** Upload by drag-drop, per-file email address, maildrop, fax or scanner → junk images filtered → AI splits signed packets and suggests checklist items → forms filled from MLS and tax data → e-sign envelope (roles, order, blocks, pre-mapped fields, client-only fields) → reminders by SMS/WhatsApp, correction in flight, certificate of completion → "signed" is kept separate from "compliant".
4. **Compliance review.** Agent submits an item ("notify office to review") → review queue across all files (Quick Audit) → AI signature/initial check and cross-document scan (Blocker / Warning / Info) → accept or reject with a required reason (templates) → agent gets a precise re-upload prompt; flagged items nag on a broker-set cadence → multi-stage, multi-tier approval → broker marks the file Compliant → commission documents and payout unlock (8.3).
5. **Closing.** Title/escrow order sent from the deal; order status returns to the timeline → lender milestones via LOS sync; financing-risk alerts (stale pre-approval, open conditions near closing) → insurance quote and binder to lender → inspection and appraisal (ROV pack) → wire-fraud controls → RON or in-person signing → Closed (blocked while the close date is in the future, TX-1.18) → status pushes to CRM, MLS and accounting → retention clock starts.
6. **Fall-through.** Deal dies → "New Offer" creates a linked transaction that copies only this side's data; the client keeps offer history (TX-1.11, TX-1.12) → unaccepted and rejected offers stored for retention.

#### States and statuses

- **Fixed status with company stages:** Active Listing · Under Contract · Closed · Void, each with named, coloured stages (Inspection, Underwriting, Clear to Close…) (TX-1.5). Per-type status sets follow dotloop's model (PRE_OFFER → UNDER_CONTRACT → SOLD → ARCHIVED; PRE_LISTING → PRIVATE_LISTING → ACTIVE_LISTING → UNDER_CONTRACT → SOLD → ARCHIVED; lease types end in LEASED).
- **Listing file:** Active · Expired (computed from dates) · Canceled/Pending · Canceled/Approved (only admin/broker can approve or reactivate).
- **Buyer Record:** Active · In Contract · Closed · Termination Requested · Terminated (7.9).
- **Envelope:** Draft · Sent/In Progress · Correcting · Canceled · Completed.
- **Document review:** Pending · Accepted · Rejected / Revision Requested · Exemption Requested · Approval revoked; checklist item levels Required · If Applicable · Optional · Private.
- **Next-action chip:** yellow = needs you · blue = waiting on others · green = done ("Road to Success", TX-1.20).

#### Notifications

Task digests, flagged-item nags, review rejections with reason (mandatory), signer reminders, deadline warnings, AI-proposed changes awaiting approval, title/lender status changes, financing-risk alerts, close-date and retention events; consumers get document requests and signing links by magic link.

#### Permissions

Five-role review model (agent, limited TC, office manager, admin/auditor, broker; only admin/auditor and broker accept or reject, close, archive or reactivate); TCs act on behalf of agents without shared logins, every action logged with both names; document permission categories (Office, Listing, Sale, Buyer, Public, Private) keep one side's documents from the other side's agent on dual files; private checklist items and personnel files admin-only; edits to closed files only through a controlled admin path.

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

AI Create and AI Update from contracts with field-level citations ($1/run benchmark, monthly cap, per-role permission); execution-date timeline rooting; corrections train the extractor; email intake that matches existing deals; automatic filing of email attachments; AI-proposed actions with Approve / Dismiss / Always approve and answer-only mode; ask-about-deals in natural language and by SMS (read-only); AI compliance scan and signature check with pre-filled rejection comments; AI email rules and auto-drafting on tasks; AI form fill from uploaded blanks; template import from a screenshot. AI never marks a file compliant, approves a document or releases money.

#### Compliance hooks

NAR settlement buyer agreements and compensation disclosures; state retention (CA B&P §10148 3 years; FL §475.5015 5 years + 2 after litigation); rejected-offer storage; ESIGN consent for signers; brokerage-required disclosures (wire fraud, compensation, dual capacity, private listing limitations); RESPA §8 and ABA disclosures on settlement-service referrals; GLBA borrower consent for loan data; appraiser independence and ROV; RON state rules; FinCEN RRE capture behind a flag; 1031 exchange deadlines (tracker only, funds stay with the QI); UK sales progression and AML; UAE RERA forms A/B/F/I and Ejari. See section 3.

#### Integrations

Association forms libraries (CAR, OREA, Florida Realtors/Form Simplicity, zipForm/TransactionDesk editions), DocuSign, MLS and tax records, Gmail/Outlook intake, Google/Outlook calendars, Encompass Partner Connect and Blend, Qualia and other title platforms, CertifID/Qualia Shield-style verification, Matic/Covered, Inspectify/Spectora, Reggora, Proof/Snapdocs RON, i1031-style QIs, Earnnest, dotloop and SkySlope importers, Zapier on status change.


### Module 8. Back office and brokerage operations

**Rows:** 114 (66 Must, 39 Should, 9 Later).

**Purpose.** Brokerage money and people operations on the same deal record: earnest money and trust accounting with three-way reconciliation, commission plans (splits, caps, sliding scales, team plans), CDAs and payouts, agent billing, 1099s, accounting and QuickBooks/Xero sync, plus recruiting, agent onboarding, training/LMS, intranet and referral tracking. Bar: BoldTrail BackOffice (Brokermint), SkySlope Books, Paperless Pipeline commissions, Lone Wolf Back Office, Real's reZEN, BrokerMetrics/Brokerkit (`features/04`, `features/07`).

**Users.** Brokers of record, office admins, accounting staff, recruiters, trainers; agents (commission view, cap progress, invoices, onboarding, courses); title/escrow as payers; referral partners and relocation companies.

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B12 | **Referral networks take a third and send poor leads** | Agents | "sends you the upset lead for 33% of your commission" ([Trustpilot HomeLight](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/homelight.com)); Offerpad "refused to honor the referral fee" after a signed 3% agreement ([Trustpilot Offerpad](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/www.offerpad.com)) | Referral agreements signed in-app, with payment instructions stored on the deal and paid through the closing disbursement. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Earnest money.** Deal records EMD amount, holder and dates → if the brokerage holds it, a deposit request goes out (digital ACH link, gated approval) → receipt issued to meet state rules → posted to the trust liability ledger per property → applied or refunded at closing (deducted from commission payable when held) → monthly three-way reconciliation (system liability = bank balance = statement, differences $0.00; follow-up log; preparer and designated broker sign; package filed).
2. **Commission to payout.** Deal compliant (7.6) → commission calculated from the agent's plan (flat/tiered gross, pre- and post-split deductions, referral, franchise fee, team allocation, cap proration mid-deal, post-cap fee) → agent validates the calculation → CDA generated on the brokerage's form, e-signed and sent to title → disbursement state machine advances on approve, confirm payment and process payouts → batch ACH (next-day) or checks; paystubs; reversible payouts → posts to the GL and QuickBooks/Xero as journal entries → cap progress and the celebratory cap closing statement update for the agent.
3. **Agent billing.** Recurring desk, tech and E&O fees and bulk one-time charges → statement cycle (cut-off, review window, send date with pay link) → auto-charge or invoice-only → unpaid balance collected from the next commission → agent pays from a self-pay portal; who pays processing fees configurable.
4. **Recruit to productive agent.** MLS production data and agent-movement alerts → recruit pipeline with fit score and a net-income pitch at our plan vs theirs → offer → onboarding checklist (licence transfer, MLS/board, W-9, E&O, ICA, IdP account, website, brand kit) auto-provisioned, onboarding packet e-signed → required compliance courses with attestations (fair housing, wire fraud, TCPA/DNC, AI policy) → licence and CE expiry tracked, blocking new listings on expiry.
5. **Departure.** Agent leaves → one-click departure workflow (13.5): brokerage-owned leads reassigned, agent-owned book exported, seat billing stopped, tokens revoked, open files reassigned with "Update Agent".

#### States and statuses

- **EMD:** expected · requested · received · held in trust · applied · refunded · released (with approval).
- **Disbursement:** Incomplete Deal → Pending Approval → Pending Payment Confirmation → Pending Remaining Payouts → Payment Processed (SSB); agent view: Needs Commission Validation → Commission Validated → Commission Document Generated → Sent → Closing Confirmed → Waiting to Settle → Payment Scheduled/Settled (REAL).
- **CDA:** draft (until approved) · approved · sent · signed · posted to file.
- **Invoice / statement:** draft · reviewed · sent · paid · partially paid · collected from commission · charged back.
- **Commission plan:** locked (no per-deal edits) or unlocked (override + recalc); plan period with chained-plan transitions.
- **Recruit:** prospect · contacted · meeting · offer · signed · onboarding · active; own agents carry a flight-risk flag.
- **Reconciliation:** open · balanced · signed by preparer · signed by broker · filed.

#### Notifications

Deposit received or overdue; reconciliation due and out of balance; next-split threshold warnings (orange) when a deal will cross a tier; CDA ready for approval; payout processed; statements sent; chargebacks; licence/E&O/CE expiry; course due; agent-movement alerts; flight-risk flags with a suggested action.

#### Permissions

Commission notes admin-only; agents see their own commission detail and cap progress; trust and payout actions need step-up verification and dual approval for bank-account changes (pain point B23); personnel files restricted; recruiting data restricted to recruiters and brokers; franchise roll-ups hide franchisee contact data unless shared.

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

AI recruit-fit, pitch builder and territory mapping (PC-12.4); flight-risk scoring on first-party activity (PC-12.5); AI Q&A over the policy library with citations (PC-14.5); AI role-play coaching (PC-13.4); month-end close-out view built through MCP (RPT). AI never approves a CDA, moves trust funds or changes a commission plan.

#### Compliance hooks

State trust-account rules and receipts, three-way reconciliation, retention of trust records (CA, FL), 1099-NEC/MISC/INT, W-9/TIN collection, compensation cap check against the buyer agreement at CDA time (TX-18.9 white space), RESPA §8(c)(3) cooperative-brokerage referral fees, UK client money and AU sales trust accounting (DEV-14.8), Canada provincial trust deposits, payroll forms (W-2, T4) later.

#### Integrations

QuickBooks Online (two-way, not SSB's one-way-once), Xero, Oracle/SAP for enterprise, Braintree or Payload payment gateways, bank feeds, NACHA/ACH payouts, eCommission/Rocket Advance commission advances, franchise reporting feeds, MLS data for production intelligence, IdP/SCIM for onboarding, LMS content.


### Module 9. Property management (LTR, STR, HOA, commercial)

**Rows:** 276 (143 Must, 93 Should, 40 Later).

**Purpose.** Property management for long-term rentals, short-term rentals, HOAs and commercial space on one trust-compliant ledger, with tenant, owner, vendor, guest and board portals. LTR must replace Buildium, DoorLoop or RentRedi for a 10–500 unit operator at launch and compete with AppFolio, Rent Manager and Yardi Breeze within a year; STR must match Guesty and Hostaway; HOA must match Buildium, AppFolio and Vantaca; UK lettings must match Goodlord, Arthur and Fixflo (`features/05`). It reads the same Person, Property and Unit records as sales, so a buyer who rents out their home becomes an Owner here without re-entry.

**Users.** Property managers, leasing agents, accountants, maintenance coordinators and in-house technicians; landlords (DIY); owners (owner portal/app); tenants and roommates (resident portal/app); applicants; vendors and contractors (vendor app); STR guests (guest app), cleaners; HOA homeowners and board members; commercial tenants.

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

| PM-9.5 | Retail cash payments | Barcode or slip payable at stores, posted automatically, no reversal risk | Must | Buildium/PayNearMe: 20,000+ locations, funds within 5 days (Buildium); RentRedi 90,000+ locations (RentRedi); Entrata MoneyGram 35,000+ (ResidentPay) | [Buildium](https://www.buildium.com/features/retail-cash-payments/) ; [RentRedi](https://rentredi.com/automate-rent-collection/) ; [ResidentPay](https://www.entrata.com/products/residentpay) |
| PM-9.6 | Partial-payment policy controls | Allow, block, or set a minimum for partial payments per lease | Must | RentRedi "block payments" (RentRedi); rmResident 1★ "can't pay unless it's the full amount" (App Store) | [RentRedi](https://rentredi.com/automate-rent-collection/) ; [App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id1540124885) |
| PM-9.7 | Split rent between roommates | Each roommate pays a portion | Must | TenantCloud (roommates) | [roommates](https://www.tenantcloud.com/roommates) |
| PM-9.8 | Flexible rent / pay-in-installments | Resident splits rent across the month while the owner gets paid in full on time | Should | Buildium/Livble (Resident Center); Flex (Flex) | [Resident Center](https://www.buildium.com/features/resident-center/) ; [Flex](https://getflex.com/) |
| PM-9.9 | Apple Pay, digital wallets, pay-by-text | Wallet and text-link payment | Should | Entrata ResidentPay (Entrata); Vantaca Pay Apple/Google Pay (Vantaca) | [Entrata](https://www.entrata.com/products/residentpay) ; [Vantaca](https://www.vantaca.com/hoa-payment-solutions-vantaca) |
| PM-9.10 (+PM-24.9) | Check scanning and lockbox | Remote deposit capture and a lockbox service for paper checks <br>**Also (PM-24.9, Lockbox check processing):** Dedicated PO boxes, same-day posting, Positive Pay; a two-step review resolves up to 80% of exceptions | Should | Entrata check scanning (ResidentPay); Vantaca Lockbox: same-day posting, Positive Pay (Vantaca) · Vantaca Lockbox (Vantaca) | [ResidentPay](https://www.entrata.com/products/residentpay) ; [Vantaca](https://www.vantaca.com/vantaca-lockbox) |
| PM-9.11 | Instant ledger posting and fast settlement | Payment posts immediately; funds settle in 1-3 days | Must | Entrata: posts instantly, settles within 72 h (ResidentPay); Rentec next-day, with same-day ACH for a fee (FAQ); Apartments.com Express Pay (Apartments.com) | [ResidentPay](https://www.entrata.com/products/residentpay) ; [FAQ](https://www.rentecdirect.com/details/easypay-faq) ; [Apartments.com](https://www.apartments.com/rental-manager) |
| PM-9.12 | NSF / returned-payment handling | Automatic fee and notice when a payment fails | Must | Buildium "Apply fees and trigger notices automatically when payments fail" (automation); Rentec NSF notice within 48 h (FAQ) | [automation](https://www.buildium.com/features/property-management-automation-software/) ; [FAQ](https://www.rentecdirect.com/details/easypay-faq) |

| ID | Feature | What it does | Priority | Benchmark leader | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PM-11.1 | Deposit ledger, separate from rent | Tracks deposits held, interest and liability | Must | Buildium "Collect and view every fee, deposit, and refund" (accounting) | [accounting](https://www.buildium.com/features/property-management-accounting/) |
| PM-11.2 | Applicant deposits refunded from escrow | Handles multiple applicants and refunds deposits from escrow | Must | AppFolio (accounting) | [accounting](https://www.appfolio.com/property-manager/accounting-reporting) |

| PM-11.5 | UK deposit protection registration | Registers with DPS, MyDeposits or TDS within 30 days, issues prescribed information, returns within 10 days | Must (UK) | GOV.UK (deposit protection); without protection the landlord can't get possession under the RRA (RRA guide) | [deposit protection](https://www.gov.uk/tenancy-deposit-protection) ; [RRA guide](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/guide-to-the-renters-rights-act/guide-to-the-renters-rights-act) |
| PM-11.6 | Deposit cap calculator (UK) | Enforces the legal caps on holding and tenancy deposits | Must (UK) | Goodlord "Built-in calculators tell you exactly how much deposits should be" (Goodlord) | [Goodlord](https://www.goodlord.com/letting-agent-solutions/tenant-payments) |
| PM-11.7 | Deposit alternatives | No deposit, a reduced deposit, installments, or an interest-bearing full deposit | Should | Obligo: four options, full deposit earns up to 2% APY (Obligo); LeaseLock Zero Deposit (LeaseLock); Entrata: "50% more coverage than a traditional security deposit" (Entrata) | [Obligo](https://www.obligo.com/) ; [LeaseLock](https://leaselock.com/) ; [Entrata](https://www.entrata.com/products/deposit-alternative) |
| PM-11.8 | Digital move-out charge flow | Move-out charges and refunds paid electronically, no paper checks | Should | Obligo (Obligo) | [Obligo](https://www.obligo.com/) |
| PM-11.9 | STR security holds | Authorization hold vs a damage deposit, max 7 days (5 on Visa), auto-release | Must (STR) | Guesty (help, automated deposits) | [help](https://help.guesty.com/hc/en-gb/articles/9363850717085-What-is-the-difference-between-an-authorization-hold-and-a-security-deposit) ; [automated deposits](https://help.guesty.com/hc/en-gb/articles/31845297472797-Managing-automated-security-deposits) |

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |

| B23 | **Trust-accounting gaps** | PMs | Buildium bank change for a trust account ">20 days… with NO RESOLUTION"; "chart of accounts is restricted to two tiers" ([Trustpilot Buildium](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/www.buildium.com)). AppFolio: "Only one bank account allowed per specific GL account number" ([TrustRadius AppFolio](https://www.trustradius.com/products/appfolio-property-manager/reviews)) | Multi-tier chart of accounts, many bank accounts per GL, same-day trust-account bank change with dual approval. |

They also raise prices regularly without warning" ([Trustpilot DoorLoop](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/doorloop.com)) | A fee ledger visible to landlord and tenant; 60-day notice on price changes; no-fee ACH option. |

There was a maintenance portal that was much easier" (2026-09-17); FolioSpace averages **2.05** across 100 reviews since 2025, 74 of them 1-2 stars ([App Store id1376350358](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id1376350358)) | Form first, with photo, location and access window; AI only assists with triage behind the scenes. |

([App Store id541403633](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/id541403633), 2026-10-03) | Grace-period auto-waiver when the platform has an outage; passkeys plus long-lived trusted device sessions. |

| B31 | **Legacy enterprise PM user experience** | PM staff | "Default page timeout duration is short… payables or journal entries"; "Menus are all over the place"; "Would like to see an Android and iOS app… geared toward accounting functions" ([TrustRadius Yardi Voyager](https://www.trustradius.com/products/yardi-voyager/reviews)) | Keyboard-first desktop, autosave drafts, and an accounting approvals mobile app. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Vacancy to lease.** Unit becomes vacant (or notice given) → listing with AI copy, published to ILSs and UK portals with two-way sync → inquiries auto-invited to self-schedule (pre-qualification gate, slot locking, reminders, no-show tracking; self-guided tours with ID check and one-time lockbox codes) → online application with save-and-resume and fees shown upfront → screening in the applicant's own app (credit, criminal, eviction, income via bank/payroll, document-fraud and ID liveness checks) against written per-property criteria → recommendation approve / conditional / deny → FCRA adverse-action notice generated whenever a report influenced the decision → lease from a state-specific template, e-signed → deposit or deposit alternative collected → move-in checklist (lease, deposit, insurance or own policy upload, utilities, autopay) with a manager progress widget → move-in inspection.
2. **Rent.** Charges post from the lease → reminders before due → resident pays by free ACH (fee-free path listed first), card, wallet, text-to-pay or retail cash, seeing the total and every fee before the pay button → split between roommates, partial payments per lease policy, flexible installments → payment posts instantly to tenant, property, owner and bank ledgers → NSF triggers fee and notice → late fees per lease terms (waivable in bulk) → delinquency dashboard → payment plan or AI collections outreach → collections hand-off. UK: unique payer reference per tenancy; possession grounds and arrears thresholds tracked.
3. **Maintenance.** Resident reports by picture-based issue picker, form, SMS or AI chat (form always available) with photos/video and access window → customer-defined emergency rules and after-hours escalation → optional AI troubleshooting before dispatch → duplicate suppression → auto-route to vendor by category (fallback marketplace) → owner approval if above threshold → vendor accepts in the vendor app, arranges access with the tenant, logs time, materials, photos and invoice → resident gets SMS status at each step and a completion survey → work order → bill → owner invoice with markup → vendor paid by ACH/virtual card/check → owner statement. UK: PPM for gas safety/EICR; Awaab's Law hazard timers.
4. **Owner month-end.** Bank feeds matched → three-way reconciliation → management fees calculated → owner distributions wizard (income − expenses − reserve → send) → scheduled, branded owner statements published to the owner portal → owner approves quotes, funds contributions, sees vacancies and work-order history; per-owner module toggles.
5. **STR stay.** Reservation arrives from Airbnb/Vrbo/Booking/Expedia/Google or the direct booking site (channel manager, rates and availability sync, per-channel overrides) → guest verified, payment split or installments, security hold → scheduled guest messages and AI replies with per-type review → smart-lock code generated after payment → cleaning and inspection tasks auto-created, rescheduled when the booking changes; property marked Dirty at check-in → checkout balance collected → host review → revenue-share owner statement; owner stays block dates with turnover tasks but no charges. Taxes synced per channel; registration numbers shown where the city requires (NYC LL18).
6. **HOA cycle.** Assessments post to homeowner ledgers → online dues → violations logged with photos (GPS map drive-bys), escalation stages and auto-mailed letters → architectural review submission → board vote → board portal for invoice approval and check e-signing → board documents and financials on demand.
7. **Commercial.** Lease types (NNN, gross, hybrid) → CAM pools with caps → estimated CAM billing → annual reconciliation and tenant recovery letters → scheduled and CPI escalations → percentage rent from tenant sales.
8. **Renewal and move-out.** Renewal likelihood predicted → bulk renewal offers with resident choice (or default auto-roll; UK Section 13 instead of rent-review clauses) → notice → pre-move-out inspection offer (CA) → move-out inspection compared side by side with move-in → itemised deposit disposition within the statutory deadline with receipts → final account → unit turn board.

#### States and statuses

- **Unit:** occupied · notice given · vacant-unrented · vacant-rented (pre-move-in) · make-ready · down/offline; listing: unlisted · listed · upcoming.
- **Application:** started (saved) · submitted · screening · criteria met / conditional / not met · approved · denied (adverse action sent) · withdrawn.
- **Lease / tenancy:** draft · out for signature · active · month-to-month / periodic (UK APT) · notice given · ended · in renewal (offer sent, accepted, declined).
- **Charge / payment:** due · paid · partial · late · NSF/returned · waived · on payment plan · sent to collections.
- **Work order:** new · triaged (emergency / urgent / routine) · awaiting approval · assigned · scheduled · in progress · completed · resident confirmed · billed · closed · reopened.
- **Deposit:** held · interest accruing · disposition drafted · returned / deducted (with itemisation) · UK: protected (scheme ref) · prescribed info served.
- **Reservation (STR):** inquiry · booked · checked in · checked out · cancelled; property cleaning status Dirty / Clean / Inspected.
- **Violation (HOA):** reported · notice 1 · notice 2 · fine · hearing · cured; ARC: submitted · under review · approved · denied · completed.
- **Owner statement:** draft · published · emailed · regenerated.

#### Notifications

Residents: rent reminders, receipts, fee disclosures, maintenance status by SMS, announcements on login, renewal offers, notices with proof of delivery. Owners: statements, approvals needed, contribution requests, vacancy and occupancy updates. Vendors: job offers, schedule, payment status. Managers: delinquency, emergencies, SLA breaches, expiring vendor COIs, expiring UK certificates, deposit deadlines, lease expirations, reconciliation exceptions. Guests: booking, door code, check-in info, checkout reminders.

#### Permissions

Custom roles with property-level scoping (e.g. maintenance staff see only their properties and no financials); per-owner portal module toggles; roommates each see their share; vendors see only their jobs and payment status; board members see association financials and approvals only; screening results field-masked from roles without need; multi-company login for staff working for several management companies.

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

AI listing description writer; AI leasing agent for 24/7 lead response and tour booking; AI application progression; AI maintenance troubleshooting, triage and work-order summaries; AI collections outreach that can offer payment plans only inside configured limits; renewal likelihood prediction; rent pricing recommendations (customer data not pooled); AI bill entry; AI month-end close and budget drafting; AI inspection reports from voice; AI owner Q&A; AI guest replies with per-type review control. Residents always have a form and a person; emergency classification follows the customer's rules, not the model.

#### Compliance hooks

FCRA adverse action and consistent screening criteria; fair-housing-safe listing copy; state deposit rules (CA 21 days, $125 receipt threshold, pre-move-out inspection offer); CA AB 2747 rent reporting offer; rent control caps; affordable housing (LIHTC, HUD 50059) later; STR registration (NYC LL18) and EU guest registration; FTC rental junk-fee rulemaking (total rent first); UK RRA (periodic tenancies, no Section 21, Section 13, possession grounds, no rental bidding, anti-discrimination, pets, PRS database, ombudsman, Decent Homes, Awaab's Law), deposit protection within 30 days, Right to Rent, How to Rent pack, safety certificates, MTD for Income Tax; 1099 e-filing and W-9 collection; trust accounting per state board. See section 3.

#### Integrations

ILSs (Apartments.com, Zillow Rental Network, Zumper, Apartment List, Craigslist), UK portals (Rightmove, Zoopla, OnTheMarket, StuRents), TransUnion, Plaid, Snappt, Stripe/PayNearMe/MoneyGram/Western Union cash networks, Flex/Livble, Obligo/LeaseLock, TheGuarantors, MSI renters insurance, HappyCo/zInspector, Fixflo/Latchel/Lula vendor networks, Conservice, deposit schemes (DPS, MyDeposits, TDS), HMRC MTD, Airbnb/Vrbo/Booking/Expedia/Google, PriceLabs, smart locks, Chekin, Breezeway, QuickBooks/Xero, Baselane-style banking.


### Module 10. Developer and new homes

**Rows:** 174 (90 Must, 53 Should, 31 Later).

**Purpose.** One unit record from launch to keys for developers and builders: project/tower/phase/unit inventory with live status and holds, versioned pricing and cost sheets, site visits, launch-day EOI and token booking with a fair queue, booking and KYC, payment plans and construction-linked demands, collections and tax, loans, cancellations and transfers, channel partners with collection-linked brokerage, customer portal, possession, snags and warranty; plus US new-homes site maps, options and design centre, and off-plan directories for brokers. Today this takes Sell.Do + Farvision in India or Lasso + Envision + BuildTopia + Avid in the US (`features/06`).

**Users.** Developer sales heads, pre-sales and site reps, launch command-centre staff, CRM/post-sales and collections teams, finance, site engineers (milestone certification), handover and snag teams, contractors; channel partners and sub-brokers (CP portal/app); buyers and co-applicants (customer portal/app); US builder sales agents, design-centre staff and co-op real estate agents.

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B34 | **Builder buyers expect customization and cost clarity** | New-home buyers, developers | 70% want full customization; top upfront worries are insurance (50%), taxes (49%) and closing costs (49%); 74% of first-time buyers worry about qualifying ([Homes.com 2026 New Construction survey](https://www.homes.com/solutions/sites/default/files/file/2026-08/HDC_NewHomes_CustomerSurveyReport_2026_DIGITAL.pdf)). Lasso CRM: "sometimes difficult to navigate for admins" ([TrustRadius Lasso](https://www.trustradius.com/products/lasso-crm/reviews?qs=pros-and-cons)) | Option and upgrade configurator with live price and payment impact; preferred-lender pre-qualification with ABA disclosure; construction-stage photo updates. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Set up a project.** Project → tower/phase/plot → floor → unit with configuration, facing, carpet area, add-ons (parking, storeroom) as inventory → versioned price list and PLCs → payment-plan templates (CLP, down-payment, flexi, time-linked) → RERA/DLD numbers, escrow account and off-plan advertising authorisation recorded → sales kit and floor plans published to reps, CPs and the off-plan directory; US: community plan portfolio, lot premiums and interactive site map.
2. **Pre-sales and site visit.** Leads from portals, ads, walk-ins, missed calls and expo QR (4.1) → routing by project, language, score and NRI desk with SLA escalation (4.2/4.3) → site visit booked with WhatsApp confirmation → geo-fenced check-in; CP-sourced visits OTP-verified to lock attribution → structured post-visit feedback → quote PDF to WhatsApp with discount approval chain inside caps.
3. **Launch day.** Tiered EOIs collected online with tokens and instant receipts → queue ordered by tier plus millisecond timestamp, visible to each buyer with honest wait → slots split for walk-ins, CPs and pre-sales → unit selection on the live grid (sub-second sync, holds with their own expiry, concurrency guard: one booking wins) → token adjusted into the ledger → command centre shows sold/released, queue, value booked, payment failures, velocity per 15 minutes, absorption by tower.
4. **Booking to agreement.** Digital booking form, cost sheet, allotment letter and receipt from one record → co-applicants with separate KYC (Aadhaar/PAN eKYC, Digilocker, penny-drop) → agreement for sale from locale templates (RERA state clauses, DLD-aware, multi-language) → e-sign and e-stamp → booking lifecycle booked → agreement → registration → possession; stalled-booking and missing-document alerts.
5. **Construction-linked collections.** Site engineer certifies a milestone → demands raised for every unit in that tower at the right %, GST and due date → bulk review then dispatch, tracked per channel (email opened, WhatsApp read, registered post) → T-7/T-3/T-1 reminders with pay links → receipts with maker-checker, escrow split on every receipt → bank-statement auto-reconciliation → ageing buckets set the reminder tone; interest on overdue per clause → bank-loan tracker prepares disbursement claims and NOCs.
6. **CP lifecycle.** CP self-onboards (OTP, KYC, RERA agent number, e-signed agreement, maker-checker) → sees live inventory through the visibility matrix (stock, not economics) → tags leads with an exclusivity window → books units and collects tokens → brokerage becomes eligible tranche by tranche as the buyer pays → CP self-invoices with TDS/GST → shared payout ledger; clawback on cancellation; sub-broker splits.
7. **Cancellation or transfer.** Reason captured → forfeit/refund computed per clause → refund schedule → unit released atomically to live inventory with re-price options → CP clawback → cancellation documents to the buyer app. Transfer/nomination of an under-construction unit with fee, new-party KYC and ledger carry-over (white space).
8. **Handover and warranty.** Guided handover checklist → snags tapped on the floor plan with photos, defect type and severity SLAs → auto work orders to the right contractor → buyer re-inspects or reopens → keys held while critical snags are open → possession → warranty clock and homeowner warranty portal → post-possession requests route to facility management.

#### States and statuses

- **Unit:** available · held (owner, expiry) · blocked (token, time-bound) · booked · agreement · registered · possession · sold; also lease, TBC, not released.
- **EOI:** submitted · token paid · ranked · allotted · converted to booking · refunded · expired.
- **Booking:** booked → agreement → registration → possession; cancelled · transferred.
- **Demand:** draft · sent · partly paid · paid · overdue (ageing bucket) · waived (credit note).
- **Loan (IN):** application → sanction → tripartite NOC → loan agreement → disbursal tranches; any step can be BLOCKING with a named owner.
- **CP:** applied · KYC pending · approved · active · tier (Gold/Platinum) · dormant · blocked; **lead tag:** tagged · extended (site visit) · expired · converted · disputed; **payout:** eligible · raised · released · on hold · clawed back.
- **Snag:** reported · assigned · fixed · buyer accepted · reopened; severity critical (48h), major (7d), minor (21d).
- **UK reservation:** reserved · cooling-off (≥14 days) · refund due · exchanged.

#### Notifications

Hold expiring (countdown chip), double-booking conflict, EOI position and wait, payment failure and retry, demand dispatched/read/overdue, receipt issued, loan step blocked, CP tag expiring in <48h, payout released, construction progress photos to buyers, snag SLA breach, possession and warranty-period reminders, NHQC cooling-off and refund deadlines (UK), NSW change notices (AU).

#### Permissions

Visibility matrix per CP field (availability ✓, price band ✓, cost sheet on hold, customer phone ✗, other partners' leads ✗); multi-project, multi-entity (SPV) isolation; discount caps by role; maker-checker on receipts and credit notes (Sales Head → Finance → CFO); field-level access policies; buyers see only their units and ledgers; MCP access for CPs scoped by role.

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

AI intent scoring and EOI intent ranking; exhaustion forecast; price-band simulation and absorption-linked price ladder (logged, reversible, price changes approved); AI quote compliance check; desk load balancing; live sell-out forecast; missing-document chaser; stalled-booking alerts; late-payer prediction; AI reconciliation suggestions with confidence for one-click confirm; AI document OCR for CP onboarding and invoices; commission Q&A assistant; AI buyer assistant; AI warranty triage; morning briefing and budget reallocation suggestions. AI never allots a unit, changes a price list, posts a receipt or approves a credit note on its own.

#### Compliance hooks

India: RERA project and agent numbers on every asset, carpet-area pricing, 70% escrow split, quarterly RERA updates, GST/TDS/26QB/Form 16B, stamp duty, e-invoicing, DLT/NDNC/DPDP. UAE: Trakheesi/Madmoun on ads, DLD developer register, one escrow per project, off-plan advertising authorisation, Oqood (not verified), RERA forms, VAT/corporate tax. UK: New Homes Quality Code (14-day cooling-off, refund clock, independent pre-completion inspection, 2-year after-sales), Consumer Code for Home Builders. AU: Victoria 10% deposit cap and sunset/registration rescission; NSW disclosure statement, 10-business-day cooling-off and 21-day change notices. US: ILSA/Reg J property reports, lien waivers before trade payments, co-op agent on the opportunity. See section 3.

#### Integrations

Indian portals (99acres, MagicBricks, Housing and 16+), Bayut and Property Finder, Meta/Google/LinkedIn lead ads with CAPI/offline conversions, WhatsApp BSPs, Indian telephony, Razorpay/PayU/Stripe/Cashfree, Aadhaar eKYC/Digilocker/NSDL e-sign/e-stamp, banks (HDFC, ICICI, SBI, Axis, Kotak panel), SAP/Oracle/Salesforce two-way, Power BI; US: Zonda site maps, NewHomeSource and Livabl, Envision/Higharc configurators, Lasso/Hyphen CRM patterns, Hyphen BuildPro/SupplyPro, AvidWarranty, Zonda UTour self-tours and kiosks.


### Module 11. Marketing, lead generation and nurture

**Rows:** 19 (6 Must, 10 Should, 3 Later).

**Purpose.** Everything that brings leads in and keeps past clients coming back, outside the single listing: lead-generation ads with honest attribution and spend ledgers, brand kits and template libraries locked by the brokerage, advertising compliance, and the homeowner/sphere nurture engine (likely-to-sell scores, AI homeowner agent, lender co-sponsorship). Listing-level marketing lives in 3.7; consumer-facing homeowner screens live in 2.6.

**Users.** Agents, team marketing staff, brokerage and franchise marketing teams (acting on behalf of agents), loan officers (co-sponsors), brokers reviewing spend.

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B10 | **Lead products bill after cancel; poor-quality leads** | Agents | "notified them to cancel IN WRITING. they charged me again the next month… billed AGAIN 60 days past my written notice"; "Lots of fake contact info" ([Trustpilot Realtor.com](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/realtor.com)). Apartments.com: "$800/month… 12-month contract… 2-3 leads a month" ([Trustpilot Apartments.com](https://www.trustpilot.com/review/apartments.com)) | Ad-spend and lead-source ROI ledger (cost per lead, per showing, per closing) and self-serve cancel. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Campaign to closing, with money shown.** Marketing creates an ad or campaign (or an MLS event triggers one, 3.7) → housing Special Ad Category enforced, no proxy exclusions → leads arrive with UTM/source (4.1) → spend pulled from ad platforms → cost per lead, per site visit/showing, per booking/closing by source, campaign and rep → closed deals and bookings pushed back to Meta CAPI and Google offline conversions → budget reallocation suggestions (Later) → transparent ad-spend ledger shows spend vs fee vs leads vs closings (`01-market-research.md` white space).
2. **Brand control.** Enterprise sets brand kits (logos, fonts, colours, disclaimers) that inherit down the org tree with locks → templates merge listing, agent and office data → one trigger produces a full kit → non-template pieces go through approval → print ordering → compliance auto-insert of brokerage name, licence numbers, Equal Housing logo and MLS disclaimer by jurisdiction.
3. **Homeowner nurture.** Closing creates the homeowner (2.6) → monthly digest and market update, scheduled live CMA → engagement plus equity and tenure produce a likely-to-sell score → owners above threshold enter an opportunity list and the weekly call list → AI homeowner agent runs stages (warming up, long-term, engaged, hand-off) across email, postcard, social and text, stopping at hand-off → "sold without you" detection flags lost business → closed deals traced back to nurture with estimated GCI (database wins).
4. **Lender co-sponsorship.** Loan officer co-brands reports → each party pays its own share of actual marketing cost → split ledger and audit log stored (RESPA).

#### States and statuses

- **Campaign/ad:** draft · pending approval · running · paused · ended · rejected by platform.
- **Template/asset:** draft · approved · locked · retired.
- **Homeowner nurture stage:** warming up · long-term · engaged · hand-off; likely-to-sell band; opted out.
- **Co-sponsorship:** proposed · active · ended, with cost-split ledger.

#### Notifications

Ad results and lead counts, platform rejections, approval requests, spend thresholds, likely-to-sell threshold crossed, CMA request or high engagement (intent signal), "sold without you" alerts, weekly call list.

#### Permissions

Brand locks per org level; marketing staff can create and send as the agent with a logged on-behalf-of actor; spend data visible to the payer and brokerage; lender partners see only co-sponsored contacts' report engagement.

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

AI-adapted social copy and creative from templates (approve before publish); AI homeowner agent (consent-gated, stops on hand-off); budget reallocation suggestions shown as recommendations; fair-housing guardrails on AI-generated copy and audiences (PC-21.3).

#### Compliance hooks

Fair housing (24 CFR 100.75 including media selection), Meta housing Special Ad Category, HUD 2024 AI ad guidance (not verified), CAN-SPAM, TCPA for SMS nurture, RESPA §8 for lender co-marketing (each pays its share), truthful valuation framing, brokerage and licence disclosures on ads.

#### Integrations

Meta, Google, LinkedIn ads and conversion APIs, Canva/Marq-style design tools, print vendors, Homebot/Fello/Cloud CMA Homebeat-style report engines, MLS data, ad-spend feeds, portals' lead and spend data.


### Module 12. Reporting and analytics

#### 12.2 Brokerage financial reporting (12 rows: 7 Must, 3 Should, 2 Later)

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B2 | **Brokerage-provided tech goes unused** | Brokers | T3 Sixty: adoption of brokerage-provided tech is "roughly 15% to 20%, compared with 40% to 50% for the strongest CRMs"; "Adoption is the product" (same source) | Built-in adoption analytics (who used what, and with what result) shown to the broker. Price by transaction, not seat. |
| B3 | **Reports across systems take weeks** | Brokers | "A lot of reports that people put together from different systems take weeks to get done." Brokers can't answer "profitability per agent, company dollar, effective commission rates, lead conversion" (same source) | Live P&L per agent, per lead source and per team from the single ledger. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Daily accountability view.** Team lead opens the agent activity report (preset questions: "How many leads have we not acted on?") → counts only manual calls, texts and emails → initially vs currently assigned leads split → speed-to-lead by agent and source → leaderboards → drill to the lead.
2. **Month-end for a broker.** Pending commissions forecast (next 30 days) → agent contribution and cap reports → income statement, balance sheet, cash flow from the GL → month-end close-out view lists open deals blocking close → scheduled PDF to partners.
3. **Ask a question.** User types "GCI by lead source this quarter for the Austin office" → semantic layer builds the query, shows it, applies permissions → chart, table, PDF or saved view → optional schedule.
4. **Export.** Data team connects a warehouse share or nightly Parquet, or a real-time event stream (13.7), with the same permission scope.

#### States and statuses

Report/view: private · shared (columns propagate) · scheduled · pinned to dashboard. Goal: on pace · behind · ahead. Agent vitals band: Poor 0–60% · At Risk 61–69% · Great 80–100% (CRM-15.8, as given).

#### Notifications

Scheduled reports by email/PDF, goal pacing alerts, threshold alerts on any metric (event-driven rules in 13.11), weekly AI audio briefing (Later).

#### Permissions

Every report respects record scope and field masks of the viewer (13.2); franchise roll-ups aggregate without exposing franchisee contacts; exports follow the export-guardrail permission (13.4).

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

Natural-language analytics with the query shown; agent-performance AI insights; weekly AI audio briefing; benchmarks against peers. AI only reads data here; any bulk action from a report goes through 6.2 approval.

#### Compliance hooks

Data minimisation in exports, audit of every export, retention of report snapshots for MLS compliance audits (PC-7.8), market stats only under MLS licence.

#### Integrations

Snowflake/BigQuery shares, Power BI, Excel/CSV/PDF, QuickBooks export, Sisu-style goal tools, MLS market stats (Spark), portal partner scorecards (Zillow Preferred).


### Module 13. Platform core

**Rows:** 131 (85 Must, 36 Should, 9 Later, 1 context).

**Purpose.** Everything under the modules that a brokerage, franchise, property manager or developer checks before signing: org tree and tenancy, roles and permissions, identity and sessions, audit and retention, data ownership and portability, RESO-native MLS ingestion and display rules, API/webhooks/MCP/Zapier and the marketplace, import and migration, billing and metering, mobile and offline, notifications, search, security and privacy, uptime and residency, white-label, localisation and accessibility (`features/07`). The data model is in section 4.

**Users.** Tenant owners and IT admins, franchise and office admins, security and compliance reviewers, integration developers and partners, every end user (sessions, notifications, mobile), support staff (consented impersonation).

#### Feature list

IDs trace back to the feature files: BP = 01-buyer-portal, SL = 02-seller-listing, CRM = 03-agent-crm, TX = 04-transactions-backoffice, PM = 05-property-management, DEV = 06-developers-international, PC = 07-platform-core, ECO = 08-ecosystem-and-pain-points. "(+…)" lists the duplicate rows merged into this one; their text is kept under **Also**.

#### Pain points this module must fix (08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md, Part B)

| # | Pain point | Who | Evidence + source | What we build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | **Too many logins; tools fine one by one, painful together** | Brokers | "It's so many logins, so many different silos… Right now, our tech is a hindrance to our business." Brokers want "one user interface", not "just a dashboard"; staff do "swivel chair integration" ([RealEstateNews on T3 Sixty report, 2026-10-05](https://www.realestatenews.com/2026/10/05/what-do-brokers-really-want-from-tech-one-simple-interface)) | One app with shared person, property and deal records. No module has its own login or its own contact table. |
| B4 | **Unpredictable seat or usage pricing** | Brokers | Brokers object "to pricing based on seats, usage or other measures that make expenses difficult to predict… greater interest in pricing tied to files, transactions or measurable outcomes" (same source) | Per-closed-file pricing option and a published price list. |
| B6 | **Learning curve and cost block new tech** | Agents | NAR 2026: biggest hurdles are learning curve (63%), cost (59%), too many options (44%), privacy/security (36%) ([RealEstateNews on NAR 2026](https://www.realestatenews.com/2026/09/22/agents-branching-out-from-generative-ai-as-adoption-grows)) | Role-based "first week" setup, templates per state, and an in-product assistant that does the task rather than explaining it. |
| B8 | **CRM deletes or loses data; restore paid and still not done** | Agents | "my account was deleted Jan.03.2024 and I lost all my leads… paid the data restoration fee… 3 month I was unable to access my database" ([TrustRadius kvCORE](https://www.trustradius.com/products/kvcore/reviews)) | Agent-owned data export at any time, point-in-time restore, and a 30-day soft-delete on all records. |

#### Key workflows

1. **Tenant and org setup.** Create tenant in a residency region → build the org tree (enterprise → brand → region → office → team; PM company, developer, CP firm, vendor as org types) → legal entity, licences and broker of record per office → settings, brand kits, display rules, retention and AI policy inherit down the tree with local overrides → roles and permission sets assigned through memberships (one user can belong to several teams or companies and switch without logging out).
2. **MLS onboarding.** Request data licence per MLS and feed type (IDX, VOW, BBO, agent/office) with status tracked in-app → adapter (Bridge, Trestle, Spark, MLS Grid, direct Web API, RETS fallback) → incremental replication on ModificationTimestamp within per-source rate limits → deletes honoured (e.g. MlgCanView=false) → media diffed and hosted on our CDN → multi-MLS de-duplication to one Property → feed-health dashboard → display rules evaluated per MLS × feed type × surface.
3. **Integration.** Partner registers an app (system key identification) → OAuth scopes ⊆ grantor permissions → REST calls with transparent rate limits and usage endpoint → signed webhooks with event IDs, retries with jitter (9 attempts / 48h), dead-letter queue and replay → MCP clients act as the user at the admin-set autonomy level → every call audited.
4. **Migration.** Choose a named migrator (FUB, kvCORE/BoldTrail, Lofty, Sierra, SkySlope, dotloop, Brokermint, AppFolio, Buildium, Sell.Do…) or CSV/XLSX mapping → dry run with dedupe preview → import as a batch with rollback token → parallel-run two-way sync during cutover → opening balances with reconciliation report.
5. **Agent departure.** Admin runs departure → ownership stamps decide: brokerage-owned leads reassigned, agent-owned contacts exported in open format to the agent, shared records copied per rule → seat billing stops → tokens and sessions revoked, offline caches wiped → audit entry.
6. **Data subject request.** Request received (CCPA/GDPR/DPDP) → identity verified → access, delete, correct, opt-out or limit, with SLA timer → cascading deletes emit events for every linked record (unlike FUB) → legal holds override deletion.

#### States and statuses

- **Membership:** invited · active · suspended · ended (with billing payer org/user).
- **Session/device:** active · trusted (remember device) · revoked · wiped.
- **Data licence:** requested · approved · active · renewal due · revoked.
- **Feed source health:** healthy · lagging · erroring · paused.
- **Webhook delivery:** pending · delivered · retrying · dead-lettered · replayed.
- **Import job:** draft mapping · dry run · running · completed · rolled back.
- **Subscription:** trial · active (monthly) · past due · cancelling at period end · cancelled; usage meters with prepaid credit balance and alerts.
- **DSR:** received · verifying · in progress · completed · rejected (reason).

#### Notifications

Owner notified of every export; security alerts (new device, MFA change, API key creation); feed-health and integration-health alerts (token expiring, leads at risk); usage-meter thresholds; billing changes with 60-day notice of price changes (pain point B25); status-page incidents per component (MLS feeds, SMS, e-sign, payments).

#### Permissions

Standard role catalogue (Owner, Admin, Broker, Office Admin, Team Lead, Agent, ISA, TC, Lender/Partner, Marketing, Accounting, PM, Property Owner, Tenant, Vendor, Channel Partner, Read-only Auditor) plus custom roles and permission sets; per-object scopes (all / team / office / own / none, include unassigned) for view, edit, create, delete; field-level masks; sensitive grants (export, delete, import, port numbers, billing, API keys, webhooks, AI enablement); "view as" simulator; delegated login only with consent and full logging.

#### AI features (human-in-the-loop per 2.0 rule 3)

Permission-inherited MCP server and API for external AI; semantic search over documents and calls (Later); sensitive-data discovery and redaction before LLM calls; AI action ledger and autonomy policy (owned by Module 6, enforced here).

#### Compliance hooks

SOC 2 Type II within 12 months; PCI-DSS scope minimisation; encryption at rest and field-level encryption for SSN/Aadhaar/PAN/bank/screening data; CCPA/CPRA with GPC; GDPR Art. 20 portability; India DPDP Rules (operational duties from about 13 May 2027); retention and legal hold per jurisdiction; WCAG 2.2 AA (DOJ Title II benchmark WCAG 2.1 AA); data residency regions (US, CA, IN, EU/UK, UAE, AU).

#### Integrations

RESO Web API and aggregators, Clareity-style MLS SSO (not verified), Okta/Entra/Google SAML/OIDC, SCIM, Zapier/Make/n8n, Stripe/Metronome billing, Gmail/Outlook, QuickBooks/Xero, DocuSign, Twilio, Meta/Google lead ads, Zillow/Realtor leads, ShowingTime, SIEM (Splunk, Datadog), warehouses, app stores for branded apps.


---

## 3. Compliance packs by country and state

Each row is a rule, what the product must do about it, the home module (section numbers from section 2), the source copied from the feature files, and whether the research verified it. Rows marked **Not verified** or **Partly** must be confirmed with counsel before the product enforces them; they are also listed in section 6. Packs switch on per tenant and per office (legal entity and jurisdiction on the OrgUnit, section 4), and every enforced rule writes to the audit log.

**How packs work in the product.** A pack is a versioned bundle of: required fields, blocking checks (e.g. "publish blocked without a Trakheesi permit"), timers and deadlines (e.g. UK deposit protection 30 days, NHQC 14-day cooling-off), document templates (notices, disclosures, agreements), tax tables, and consent rules. Packs never fork the data model; they add fields, checks and templates to the same records.

| Country / state | Rule | What the product must do | Module | Source | Verified? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 4. Core data model

One graph, modules on top. Every module reads and writes the same business objects (4.1); the platform layer (4.3, from `features/07-platform-core.md`) wraps them with tenancy, permissions, consent, audit, MLS provenance, integrations, analytics and billing. Rules that tie them together are in 4.2 and at the end of 4.3.

### 4.1 Business objects

| Object | What it is | Key fields | Key relations | Main modules |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Person** | Any human: lead, buyer, seller, tenant, owner, guest, vendor contact, CP, recruit, agent's client | names, phones/emails/handles (normalised), languages, roles[], lead stage, segments, tags, scores (lead, intent, likely-to-sell, flight-risk for own agents), KYC/AML/ID status, custom fields, OwnershipStamp | member of Household; party to Deal/Lease/Booking; has ConsentRecords, Conversations, Tasks; linked Persons (relationship labels) | 4, 2, 3, 9, 10 |
| **Household** | People who buy, sell or rent together (spouse, co-buyer, roommates, family) | members with relationship, primary contact, shared timeline | many Persons; one search/board; Deals and Leases | 4, 2, 9 |
| **Organization** (business object view of OrgUnit) | Brokerage, team, PM company, developer, CP firm, vendor, lender, title company, HOA, RMC | legal name, licences, RERA/DED/agent numbers, COI/W-9, bank details (masked) | OrgUnit tree; employs Users; party to Deals; vendor on WorkOrders | all |
| **Property** | A physical asset or parcel | address (normalised), UPI/parcel, geo, facts, tax history, AVM history, risk layers, documents | has Units; many ListingRecords (multi-MLS dedupe); Deals; Leases; Homeowner record; Project link | 1, 3, 9, 10 |
| **Unit** | A sellable or rentable part of a Property, or of a Project (apartment, floor-plate unit, lot/plot, room, bed, parking bay, storeroom) | configuration (beds/BHK), area (carpet/sqft), facing, floor, add-ons, status machine, hold (owner, expires_at), price version | belongs to Property and/or Project tower/phase; one live inventory engine for launch grid, site map, rental vacancy and STR calendar | 10, 9, 1 |
| **Listing** | An offering of a Property or Unit for sale, lease or short stay | type (sale/lease/STR), status, exempt type and `delay_until`, seller certifications, price and history, media, distribution matrix per channel, permits (Trakheesi, RERA), display flags (opt-outs), showing rules, offer instructions, public-marketing log | Property/Unit; seller Persons; Showings; Offers; marketing Assets; SyndicationJobs; becomes Deal on acceptance | 3, 1, 9 |
| **Showing / Tour / SiteVisit** | One appointment to see a property | type, purpose (showing, inspection, appraisal, walk-through, photography), slot, access window and codes, requester, attendees, status, feedback, geo check-in, OTP | Listing/Unit; Persons; Deal; CP attribution | 3, 2, 9, 10 |
| **Offer** | A proposed set of terms from a buyer side | price, EMD, financing, contingencies with days, dates, concessions, compensation request, escalation, inclusions, documents, status, read receipts | Listing; buyer Persons and agent; becomes the Deal contract; rejected offers retained | 3, 2, 7 |
| **BuyerAgreement** | Written buyer representation (touring or exclusive) | type, begin/end dates, area, compensation (amount, form), status, signed document, termination request | Person/Household; agent; gates Showings; feeds CDA cap check | 7, 2, 4 |
| **Deal** | One file per transaction: buyer side, listing side, both, lease, referral, BPO, new-home booking | representation type, fixed status + company stage, key dates (execution root), parties and roles, checklist, documents, envelopes, compliance state, commission data, EMD, closing services orders, timeline | Listing/Unit; BuyerAgreement; Offer; Ledger entries; Conversation; becomes Homeowner record on close | 7, 8, 2, 3, 10 |
| **Booking** (developer deal subtype) | A unit sale under a project | EOI tier/token, applicants with KYC, cost-sheet version, payment plan, agreement, registration, loan tracker, CP attribution, cancellation/transfer | Unit; Project; Demands; customer Ledger; CP payout | 10 |
| **Project** | A developer phase, tower or community | towers/phases/floors, price-list versions, payment-plan templates, RERA/DLD numbers, escrow account, milestones and certifications, sales kit | Units; Bookings; CP agreements; construction milestones | 10 |
| **Lease / Tenancy / Reservation** | Right to occupy a Unit for a period | type (fixed, periodic/UK APT, by-the-bed, commercial NNN/gross, STR reservation), residents and guarantors, rent and charges, deposit, escalations/CAM, renewal state, notices | Unit; tenant Persons; owner; Ledger; WorkOrders; Inspections | 9 |
| **Application** | Rental application and screening | applicants, fee, screening results (masked), criteria result, adverse-action notice | Listing (rental); becomes Lease | 9 |
| **WorkOrder / Snag / WarrantyClaim** | A maintenance, defect or warranty job | category, severity/emergency rule, photos, access window, vendor, approvals, time/materials, bill link, status, SLA | Unit; requester; Vendor; Ledger; Inspection | 9, 10 |
| **Inspection** | Move-in/out, periodic, safety, QC or handover inspection | template, rooms, photos, signatures, comparison links | Unit; Lease; WorkOrders | 9, 10 |
| **Ledger / Account / Entry** | Double-entry books: operating, trust (per owner/property/client), commission, collections, escrow (RERA/DLD), agent ledgers | chart of accounts (multi-tier), entries, bank accounts (many per GL), reconciliations, receipts, demands, invoices, payouts | Deals, Leases, Bookings, Persons, Organizations | 8, 9, 10 |
| **Payment** | Money in or out on the rails | method (ACH, card, wallet, cash network, UPI, NEFT), fees shown and who pays, status, settlement, chargeback/NSF | Ledger entry; payer/payee; Deal/Lease/Booking | 8, 9, 10, 2 |
| **CommissionPlan / CDA / Payout** | How agents are paid | plan type, tiers, cap, base, period, deductions, team allocation; CDA versions; disbursement status | Agent User; Deal; Ledger | 8 |
| **Conversation / Message / Call** | Every message and call | channel, direction, participants, visibility (all / agents only / private), consent check id, delivery state, recording/transcript/summary | attached to any objects it concerns (Person, Deal, Listing, Lease, WorkOrder, Booking) | 5, all |
| **Task / Appointment / Plan** | Work items and automations | type, assignee, due (relative anchors), dependencies, outcome; action plans and flows | any object | 4, 7, 9 |
| **Document / Envelope** | Files and signing | type, permission category, versions, hash, retention class; envelope signers, blocks, status, certificate | any object | 7, all |
| **Homeowner record** | The post-close relationship | AVM and tune value, equity, loan estimate, digest engagement, likely-to-sell score, documents archive | Property; Person/Household; created by Deal close | 2, 11 |
| **Campaign / Asset** | Marketing items | template, brand kit, channel, spend, results, approvals, public-marketing events | Listing; Person segments; lead sources | 3, 11 |

### 4.2 Lifecycle rules across objects

1. A closed buy-side **Deal** creates a **Homeowner record** on the **Property** for the buyer **Household** (2.6) and moves the Persons to past-client stage.
2. A homeowner who rents the home out becomes the **Owner** on a **Lease** for that Property (9.13), with no re-entry; the rent-vs-sell estimate (BP-P7) is the hand-off.
3. A **Booking** on a **Project Unit** is a Deal subtype and opens a collections **Ledger**; a CP payout is computed from the same receipts.
4. An accepted **Offer** converts the **Listing** file into the **Deal** (7.1); the seller portal switches to milestones.
5. A **BuyerAgreement** gates **Showings** (configurable) and caps compensation at CDA time.
6. Unit availability is one engine: a Unit cannot be booked, held, leased and reserved at the same time; the concurrency guard applies to all four.
7. Every business record carries one **OwnershipStamp** (`agent / team / brokerage / shared`) that drives visibility, export and departure.
8. Every outbound message, automation, AI action and API send passes the **ConsentRecord / SuppressionEntry** gate.

## 5. Release plan

**Principles.** (1) A module ships only when every Must row in its table ships on web and mobile; partial modules are not sold. (2) The US launches first; country packs follow on the same core. (3) Waves are ordered by dependency: nothing in a later wave is needed by an earlier one, and each wave unlocks a persona bundle that can be sold on its own. (4) Should rows form v1.1 (the first year); Later rows are the roadmap. IDs below are the home-row IDs from section 2 (merged duplicates travel with their home row).

### 5.1 MVP: every Must row, in eight waves

| Wave | What ships | Persona bundles it unlocks |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Platform core: org tree, roles, identity, audit, ownership, MLS ingestion and display rules, API/webhooks/MCP, import, billing and meters, mobile/offline shell, notifications, search, security; consent gate; AI governance | None alone; required by all |
| 2 | Website and search, CRM and routing, unified inbox and dialer, AI ISA and copilot, marketing basics, buyer portal (collections, readiness, touring) | **Solo agent**, **Team** (front office) |
| 3 | Seller portal and listing management (CMA, net sheet, compliance, syndication, marketing, showings, open houses, offers), buyer offer-to-close and homeowner, transactions and compliance review, closing services | **Team** complete; **Transaction coordinator**; listing-side of **Brokerage** |
| 4 | Back office (EMD and trust, commissions, CDAs, payouts, agent billing, 1099s, accounting sync, recruiting, onboarding, LMS, intranet) and reporting | **Brokerage / franchise** |
| 5 | Property management for long-term rentals with tenant, owner and vendor portals | **Property manager / landlord** (LTR) |
| 6 | Developer and new homes (inventory, pricing, launch, booking, collections, CP portal, customer portal, handover; US site maps and options) | **Developer / builder** (India-ready core and US new homes) |
| 7 | Asset packs: short-term rentals, HOA, commercial | **STR operator**, **HOA manager**, commercial landlord |
| 8 | Country packs: UK, UAE, India, Australia, Canada | **Regional broker**, UK letting agent, UAE broker/developer, India developer, AU agency |

Wave counts and the complete ID list per wave follow. Rows with a country or asset qualifier are pulled out of waves 1–6 into waves 7–8.

### 5.2 v1.1 (first 12 months after each wave): every Should row

v1.1 deepens each bundle to win against the mid-market leaders: for agents and teams, AI voice ISA, Cowork-style agentic copilot, power dialer, template-performance scores, RCS, video texting; for listings, live CMA editing, pricing benchmarks, staged launch, AI video, ad boosts, offer registration and risk checks; for brokerages, multi-stage review, AI audit panel, chained plans, native full accounting, NL analytics, recruiting intelligence and LMS; for PMs, AI leasing and maintenance agents, fraud-first screening, deposit alternatives, flexible rent, budgeting, vendor bidding, UK rent-protection insurance; for developers, the fair queue and launch command centre, dynamic price ladder, AI reconciliation, CP tiers and field-force cockpit, customer CX hub and US options configurator; for the platform, SCIM, warehouse connectors, real-time stream, marketplace, developer portal, trust centre, white-label portals. These ship in the order of the waves they extend.

Persona effect: Team, Brokerage, PM and Developer bundles reach parity with the mid-market and enterprise leaders named in each module (FUB Pro/Platform, Lofty, BoldTrail + BackOffice, AppFolio Plus, Sell.Do + IRIS).
