## 6. Open questions and not-verified list

Everything below was flagged by the research as unconfirmed. Treat each item as a hypothesis to check before the product enforces it, quotes it in marketing, or depends on it for an integration. Items are merged from all inputs and grouped by file; the market-research and plan items come first.

### From 01-market-research.md (Known gaps, condensed without changing the claims)

- Web search was unavailable; evidence comes from vendor pricing pages, App Store reviews, GOV.UK, NAR and Wikipedia.
- Unconfirmed: Zillow Premier Agent and Flex pricing and referral fees (often quoted at about 35–40%); Realtor.com/Opcity terms; third-party quotes for quote-only prices (Lofty from about $449, BoldTrail from about $499, CINC about $899–1,500 plus ad spend, Ylopo from about $395, AppFolio about $1.40–1.50 per unit, Brokermint and Open to Close tiers).
- Unconfirmed feature tiers and modules: dotloop, Lone Wolf (Transact, zipForm, BackOffice, Brokermint), Open to Close, Compass, Real's reZEN/Leo, eXp, BoomTown, Sierra, Luxury Presence, Cloud CMA, RPR; a possible 2025 Lone Wolf–MoxiWorks combination (only verified fact: MoxiWorks' founder joined Lone Wolf in July 2025); Homebot ownership and prices.
- Detailed NAR settlement terms and vendor implementations; final outcome of Compass v. Zillow; 2025 Clear Cooperation changes (later partly covered in features/02 and 07).
- Property management: RealPage/DOJ settlement terms, HUD screening guidance, state trust-accounting rules, Tenant Fees Act deposit cap, later Renters' Rights Act phases, module lists for Hostaway, Fixflo, Alto, Reapit (partly covered later in features/05 and 06).
- Developers and other regions: NoBroker, Housing.com, 99acres and MagicBricks builder packages; Salesforce builder implementations; Property Finder, Bayut and dubizzle pro tools; Trakheesi/Madmoun (later verified in features/06); off-plan payment-plan and escrow modules; PropertyGuru, Agentbox, REA and Domain agent products; Lasso and BuildTopia features; design-centre selection tools; no developer-grade seat price in INR, AED, AUD or USD. LeadSquared real-estate list excluded. Sell.Do "inventory sold" figure garbled.
- Pain-point evidence is mostly US App Store reviews (G2, Capterra, TrustRadius and Reddit blocked in round 1); prevalence, churn rates, agent-count trends and tech spend are unmeasured.
- Vendor claims not audited: Ylopo's 48% reply rate, Homebot ROI, IRIS's 400 units in 6 hours, dotloop's 50% transaction share, MoxiWorks' 94% retention.

### Open product questions raised by this spec

1. Does Estate OS become a licensed insurance agency (as Matic is) or only partner? (`features/08` A0.)
2. Build vs partner for AI voice ISA (Structurely/21X) and for wire-fraud verification (CertifID/Qualia Shield-style).
3. Which AVM to license for estimates (BP-D55) and which title fee schedules for net sheets (SL-2.10) in each state.
4. Payment processor and gateway choice for brokerage (Braintree vs Payload, TX-12.11) and PM (Stripe-class), and whether to offer built-in banking (PM-26.9).
5. Which association forms libraries can be licensed at launch (TX-3.1) and how per-state checklists are authored (pain point B32).
6. Pricing model per persona (seat, unit, file, project) and the published price list; the spec assumes month-to-month and in-app cancel.
7. Whether the compensation-cap check at CDA time (TX-18.9) and transfer/nomination of under-construction units (DEV-8.9) are truly white space or just unadvertised.
8. Which escalation-clause, appraisal-gap and multiple-counter workflows to support in offers (not documented by any verified vendor).


### From features/01-buyer-portal.md

- **Escalation clause in a consumer offer builder (O10).** No benchmark page confirmed a buyer-facing escalation clause field. It exists in state forms and agent tools, but consumer UI is unconfirmed.
- **Compass Collections specifics** (comment threads, reactions, agent notes). Compass One confirms "organize, share, and comment" ([Compass One](https://one.compass.com/)). The old /collections/ page now returns 404, so the finer details are unverified.
- **Zillow's earlier "shopping partner" removal.** Seen only in a Reddit snippet inside a Brave result; the page was not read.
- **Redfin's current offer flow details** (fields in the questionnaire, Redfin offer-strength tools). The help article is thin, and the Redfin newsroom and redfin.com pages were blocked by bot protection.
- **Realtor.com co-buyer linking mechanics, RealEstimate, My Home dashboard and Homes.com co-shop mechanics** come from App Store descriptions only. realtor.com and homes.com blocked page reads.
- **Zillow closing dashboard scope** ("Built with Spruce", title and escrow tasks). Confirmed in the press release only; Spruce's site did not resolve.
- **Knock, HomeLight and Flyhomes fees and eligibility.** Only marketing pages were read. Fee figures were deliberately left out.
- **Offerpad buyer perks beyond "early access to Offerpad owned homes" and the agent offer portal.**
- **Bayut TruCheck, TruEstimate, Dubai Transactions** come from the App Store description only; bayut.com blocked.
- **UAE Trakheesi / permit display and India RERA ID display** as listing requirements: regulatory facts not re-checked this round.
- **Rightmove** features beyond its App Store description (AI keywords, instant valuation, renter checklists). Its help pages failed to load.
- **WhatsApp as a deal-thread channel at named vendors.** Not confirmed on a vendor page this round. It is included (X2) because the market research shows WhatsApp as the default channel in India and the UAE.
- **Final walk-through checklist (C31), what-to-bring card (C33), plain-language offer summary (O12), scoped guest roles (X6), offline tour mode (X23), anniversary/PMI reminders (P16).** These are inferred gaps, not observed in any benchmark.
- **Divvy Homes.** The site now only shows a portal sign-in. Its status as an active rent-to-own provider is unverified, so it is excluded from the benchmarks.


### From features/02-seller-listing.md

- **Zillow LAS enforcement detail.** The three-strike system, 48 banned listings (43 from Compass), 1,200+ notices and the violation-type percentages come only from a vendor blog (open-homes.com). zillow.com pages were bot-blocked. The 2026 revision ("broadly accessible", MLS optional, Zillow Preview) is from RealEstateNews, not read from Zillow directly.
- **Virtual-staging rules per MLS** (Stellar, CRMLS, ARMLS) and the California AB 723 requirements come from vendor guides (stagemls.com, roomstage.ai), not the boards' rule pages or the statute text. MLS Now Rule 3.3 was read first-hand.
- **Meta Special Ad Category specifics** (15-mile minimum radius, no age, gender or zip targeting, Variance Reduction System) come from a secondary blog. Meta's own page only confirms the category is mandatory and restricts targeting.
- **Fello pricing** ($165–$799/month, 12-month contracts) is from a Luxury Presence blog, not Fello.
- **Cloud CMA LiveCMA** (editing price and net sheet live) is from a search snippet; the community page needs a login.
- **RPR Refine Value and Seller's Report internals.** The narrpr.com blog was bot-protected, so the claims come from the MLSListings and NAR summaries.
- **SkySlope Offers** beyond "compare, accept, counter, share with sellers, broker stats". skyslope.com was bot-protected.
- **Zillow Showcase capture requirements** (HDR, Zillow 3D Home, approved photographer, 10 or more photos) come from a photographer's blog. Performance claims (79% more views and so on) are Zillow's own marketing.
- **Homebot's 89% likely-to-sell accuracy** and **MoxiPresent's 3.5x sold listings** are vendor claims.
- **Open-house app pricing and features** (Curb Hero offline mode and 6,000+ integrations, Spacio from about $25/month) come from a competitor's comparison page (openhousesheet.com).
- **HomeLight Listing Management "Listing Studio"** is marked "coming soon" on its page.
- **Lone Wolf offer management**: no Lone Wolf offer-comparison product was found; Lone Wolf's offer coverage here is via Cloud CMA and dotloop-style flows only. **Canva** template-lock specifics for brokerages were not read in help docs.
- **Escalation-clause calculators, appraisal-gap fields and multiple-counteroffer workflows**: no verified vendor page documents them; they are recommended from domain knowledge.
- **Federal lead-based paint disclosure, state seller disclosure forms, RESPA limits on lender co-marketing, and TCPA consent for SMS nurture** are included as compliance needs but were not re-read for this pass.
- **Dubai QR circular content** loaded only as a title; the permit e-service steps were verified.


### From features/03-agent-crm.md

- **FCC one-to-one consent rule status.** RG's Jan 27, 2025 article says the FCC postponed it to "January 26, 2026, or sooner". SI's 2026 article still describes it as effective. We believe the 11th Circuit vacated it in *Insurance Marketing Coalition v. FCC* (Jan 2025), but did not fetch the court or FCC order this session. Confirm with counsel and the FCC docket before writing consent copy.
- **Exact FCC revocation deadline.** We believe it is 10 business days, plus a delayed "revoke-all" provision. The Federal Register abstract confirms only "any reasonable manner" and "timely".
- **State mini-TCPA quiet hours and call caps** (e.g. FL, OK, MD, WA). Not fetched.
- **Speed-to-lead statistics** (e.g. "21x more likely within 5 minutes", the MIT/InsideSales study). Not fetched. Only vendor guidance ("within 5 minutes", SI) is cited.
- **Vendor pricing** for FUB, Lofty and BT not shown above. Calling and texting add-on prices come from the earlier notes file, not re-fetched.
- **CINC features** beyond AutoTracks, AI FAQs and Flair Labs. Most CINC help pages are a Salesforce community that renders client-side. The routing, Switchboard Sarah and dialer specifics were not read.
- **Brivity, Ojo, Verse.io, LionDesk, BoomTown and Ylopo's "Raiya" branding.** No help pages were reachable or fetched.
- **Ylopo AI text reply-rate claims** (48%, from earlier notes) and the Structurely/21X conversion claims. Vendor marketing only.
- **Whether FUB's iPhone app requires a Zillow Pro account to sign in.** A single App Store review claims this; FUB help says the Zillow Pro rename "changes nothing".
- **Lofty Three-Line Dialer.** The help page says it is "not currently available to be purchased". Whether a new Lofty AOS power dialer replaces it was not confirmed. The "Power Dialer" add-on is listed on the pricing page per the earlier notes.
- **BoldTrail Concierge AI and MCP availability** outside "select agents" and eXp.


### From features/04-transactions-backoffice.md

- **dotloop:** product tiers, compliance review queue, task templates, mobile features and pricing. The site returned a PerimeterX "press & hold" challenge and the help center needs a login. Only the public API v2 was read.
- **Lone Wolf help center:** the Salesforce community was not navigated. **Lone Wolf release notes:** February–May 2026 failed to load. Back Office numbers (transactions processed, commissions processed) appeared as empty counters. **Lone Wolf and MoxiWorks:** whether they merged is still unverified.
- **Pricing:**
  - BoldTrail BackOffice package prices: the comparison page wasn't read.
  - Paperless Pipeline eSign price per 10-request block: not shown on the page read.
  - Loft47: per-agent price. Only "$47 per deal" for the managed service is confirmed.
  - SkySlope DigiSign and Books add-on prices.
- **Xero:** no vendor page read confirms a Xero integration for any back office in this set. Every confirmed integration is QuickBooks Online.
- **1099 e-filing:** no vendor confirmed IRS e-filing. SkySlope Books says explicitly that it does not file.
- **In-person or kiosk signing** (one device, several signers in turn): not confirmed for DigiSign, Authentisign, ListedKit or dotloop.
- **AI audit in states beyond CA and AZ:** SkySlope Smart Assist availability elsewhere is unconfirmed.
- **Compensation cap at disbursement:** no vendor was found checking the buyer-agreement compensation cap at CDA time. This is inferred white space, not confirmed absence.
- **SkySlope Offers and ShowingTime Offer Manager feature detail:** the SkySlope Offers page returned a bot check. ShowingTime's offer comparison features were not read beyond the bundle price.
- **Side (SideOS):** back-office specifics beyond marketing ("save agents 4 to 6 hours per transaction") were not read. The support site returned 403 to curl.
- **Earnnest:** fees, settlement time and gated-payment details beyond the product page were not captured. The FAQ page rendered empty.
- **Retention rules beyond CA and FL:** for example Texas and New York.
- **Vendor-claimed figures, not audited:** PP's "10–12 min → 2–3 min," Transactly's "90% of closing tasks," Trackxi's "4X faster," SSB's "next-day" payouts and Authentisign's "30M signatures."


### From features/05-property-management.md

- **AppFolio per-unit pricing**, Rent Manager, Entrata, RealPage, Hostaway, Vantaca and Fixflo prices: quote-only; nothing fetched.
- **AppFolio help-center (help.appfolio.com) and Buildium help-center (help.buildium.com) articles.** Both need a login or JS that the reader couldn't render, so the exact late-fee settings (grace period, caps, daily max) and the partial-payment toggles were not confirmed on help pages. The late-fee rows rely on marketing pages (AppFolio, TurboTenant, TenantCloud, RentRedi).
- **Blocking ACH or checks after NSF ("certified funds only")**: common in PM software but not confirmed on any fetched page; removed from the tables.
- **Automated fair-housing ad-copy checker**: no vendor page confirmed one. The fair-housing pages fetched were policy statements only.
- **HUD 2024 screening and advertising guidance and its 2025 withdrawal**: the HUD PDF URL returned an error page.
- **Other US state deposit rules** beyond California (NY, TX and others): the NY AG and Texas pages were blocked or empty, and the Rentec state-law blog sat behind bot protection (not bypassed).
- **RRA dates after 1 May 2026** (PRS database launch, ombudsman start, Decent Homes timing, Awaab's Law in the PRS): the GOV.UK roadmap and landlord-guidance URLs returned "page not found" or only a landing page. The RRA guide confirms these are in the Act, but not their start dates.
- **Tenant Fees Act deposit caps** (5 weeks' rent under £50k annual rent; 1 week holding deposit): the guidance page fetched was the archived index. Caps were not quoted from source.
- **Vantaca board voting and elections** beyond "voting tools", and HOA reserve-study features: not seen on fetched pages.
- **Rently, Turno, Rentcheck, PayYourRent and Bilt feature details**: pages rendered too little text (JS or redirect) to cite specific features.
- **Statistics in the tables are vendor marketing claims**: AppFolio's 73%, 20% and 2.8% NOI; DoorLoop's 80% and 3.5 h; Hostaway's 25.1% and 70%; Entrata's 40% and 350% ROI; Latchel's ~20% and 50%; Second Nature's 64-point boost. None is independently audited.
- **App Store recency samples** (n ≤ 150) skew negative and are not representative of overall satisfaction. All-time averages are 4.3-4.8★ for the same apps.


### From features/06-developers-international.md

- **India RERA section details**: Sec 13 (10% advance cap before the agreement for sale), Sec 14 (5-year structural defect liability), Sec 18 (refund with interest on delay), Sec 4(2)(l)(D) (70% to a separate account withdrawn per certified progress), Sec 11 (quarterly updates). Indiankanoon, IndiaCode and ClearTax did not load. Only the Wikipedia summary (70% wording, carpet area, agent registration) and MahaRERA's "Updates (Quarterly/Annual)" menu were read.
- **UAE**: Oqood's legal basis ("Law No. 13 of 2008" appeared only in a Brave AI summary), escrow release percentages, and the meaning of the 5% retention. Only DLD FAQ question titles were read, not the answers. Off-plan payment-plan norms (60/40, post-handover plans) were not found on any vendor page. Abu Dhabi (ADREC/DARI) was not researched.
- **Property Finder Expert full feature set**: propertyfinder.ae returned 500. Only the App Store text and partner-hub marketing were read. Bayut quotas and pricing, and dubizzle Pro, were not sourced.
- **Lasso CRM and BuildTopia**: ECI pages are behind Cloudflare. Features come from SoftwareOne, SoftwareFinder and a Brave summary; prices are unknown. "Model home kiosks" capture for Lasso is from a Brave summary of lassocrm.com.
- **NewHomeSource and Livabl pages**: blocked by press-and-hold bot checks. Claims come from Zonda's own listings pages.
- **Salesforce for real estate**: three Salesforce URLs returned 404. Its description comes only from Sell.Do's comparison page ("USD 25–330/user/mo", Sell.Do's own note says to verify).
- **Kylas real-estate page (404), LeadSquared developer specifics** beyond its generic real-estate CRM page, **NoBroker builder (404), Housing.com builder, 99acres builder packages (404), PropertyGuru AgentNet (login wall), REA/Domain developer products (404)**.
- **UK**: Material Information rules for listings (the NTSELAT and GOV.UK pages 404'd), HMRC estate-agency AML guidance (404), Rightmove/Zoopla/OnTheMarket developer feed specs (pages 404'd or blocked). Alto (Zoopla) was blocked by bot verification.
- **Australia**: AML/CTF "tranche 2" start date for agents; Reapit's AML page 404'd.
- **Unit transfer/assignment/nomination workflows** (Section 8): no vendor page described a full flow.
- **Vendor metrics** are vendor claims, not verified. Examples: Sell.Do 0.4% payment failures, 4% realisation lift, drop-off from 22% to 6%; Farvision 98.4% on-time demands; Envision +35% options; Avid −35% workload; UTour 3–6 months faster.
- **Market-share claims conflict**: Reapit ANZ says "66% of agencies" while its Agentbox page says "60% market share", and MRI Vault claims more than 1 in 4. These are vendor statements and cannot all be read as share of the same market.
- **App ratings** were checked with the iTunes API (AE store, Oct 2026):
  - PF Expert 4.9 (282)
  - Profolio 4.9 (3,589)
  - PropSpace 4.0 (18)
  - Goyzer CRM 4.3 (6)
  - Sell.Do 4.5 (2) in AE, 3.5 (18) in IN
  - MRI Vault and Rex Pocket show no AE ratings

  These are store aggregates, not review analysis.


### From features/07-platform-core.md

Each item below came up in research but could not be confirmed from a primary source during this run (Brave search hit bot checks; several vendor sites blocked or 404'd). Treat them as hypotheses.

- **Clareity SSO** capabilities, pricing and MLS coverage (site failed TLS). The MLS-dashboard SSO row is a hypothesis.
- **ListHub's current status** (2025–26). Only 2015–2024 snippets were found; the syndication-hub row relies on that historic model.
- **"Real Estate Exchange"** (named in the brief): could not identify which entity or product is meant; nothing sourced.
- **Aspen** is verified only as Lone Wolf's AI teammate in BrokerMetrics Talent. If the brief meant a separate "Aspen" analytics product, that is unverified.
- **Compass platform** feature set (Compass One/AI tools), **eXp** tooling, and **Real reZEN** features: pages 404'd or returned only legal footers. The rev-share tree row is inferred.
- **SkySlope** security and certifications (site blocked); **dotloop** features (blocked); **Lone Wolf BrokerMetrics/Back Office** product pages (404) beyond the press release.
- **BoldTrail SOC 2 Type II, "99.98% Actual Uptime", "100M+ API calls/month"** come from a search-result snippet of developerhub.boldtrail.com. The fetched page text did not show those figures.
- **BoldTrail brokerage-branded mobile app / white-label** is unconfirmed.
- **Zillow Listing Access Standards 2026 text**: the Zillow page did not render. Content is from search snippets (17 Mar 2026 update) plus Real Estate News and Mike DelPrete.
- **HUD 2024 AI advertising guidance**: PDF fetch timed out, so details come from search snippets. HUD's 2025–26 stance on this guidance (possible withdrawal) is unverified.
- **TCPA revocation rule** specifics (10-business-day processing; partial delay of the "revoke all" provision to 2026): only the FCC's "any reasonable manner" consumer page was verified.
- **Colorado AI Act**: the delay to 30 Jun 2026 is verified (SB25B-004). Any further 2026-session amendment or delay is unverified.
- **India DPDP Rules** dates come from search snippets (PIB, DLA Piper, MeitY listing). The primary PDF did not load. Cross-border transfer restrictions are unverified.
- **ADA Title III** (private-business website) obligations: only the DOJ Title II rule for governments was verified. WCAG 2.2 spec page did not render; the 2.2 target is a recommendation.
- **NAR IDX policy statement 7.58** and **VOW policy** pages failed to load. Exact required display elements (attribution placement, refresh intervals) vary by MLS and are not enumerated here.
- **Salesforce Hyperforce** residency specifics (page returned no text); Salesforce/HubSpot admin patterns are cited from HubSpot docs and Shield only.
- **Trestle and Spark rate limits** and **RESO UPI construction details** were not retrieved.
- **Brokerkit, Sisu, Knolyx pricing**, and whether Lone Wolf or BoldTrail ship a native LMS: unverified.
- **AppFolio Open API and Entrata API** specifics beyond `01`; **MoxiWorks Realtime Stream** event schema (only named in docs).
- A possible **Lone Wolf–MoxiWorks combination** (still unverified, as in `01`).


### From features/08-ecosystem-and-pain-points.md

- **Opendoor's agent programme change:** that the "Key Agent Program… was retired in May 2026" comes from a search snippet of help.opendoor.com. The page did not render.
- **Federal Register pages:** the 2023 CFPB opinion on comparison-shopping platforms and the 2010 HUD home warranty rule failed to load or returned 404 in the headless browser. Their content comes from search snippets, plus the Consumer Finance Monitor article (read) confirming the 2023 opinion was not rescinded. Its status after May 2025 was not checked again on consumerfinance.gov, whose withdrawn-guidance list loads dynamically.
- **State law:** whether paying referral fees to unlicensed parties (iBuyer or insurance referrals) is legal varies by state and was not researched. The same goes for state rules on agents referring inspectors, and TILA/Reg Z rules on advertised rates.
- **Encompass Developer Connect access:** "intended for … Encompass clients only" is from a search snippet; the mortgagetech.ice.com page returned 404. Partner Connect (EPC) onboarding terms and costs are unverified.
- **CertifID:** "$5M direct insurance" appears in ad copy only. The 2026 State of Wire Fraud report is gated behind a form; only the 2025 consumer findings were read.
- **Compass Concierge terms:** the $25k flat-fee tier and the up-to-$75k tier at 9.99% + 2.99% come from one Compass agent's page (Seattle). They may differ by market or have changed.
- **Knock pricing:** 2.25% + $1,850 is from Real Estate Witch (Jun 2026), not Knock's own site.
- **Relocation referral fees (35-40%):** from a JustAnswer anecdote and a third-party site snippet. No RMC rate card was verified.
- **Inside Mortgage Finance pre-approval survey:** date and sample size unknown (paywalled).
- **Rent-to-own:** the Divvy and Home Partners outcomes come from search snippets plus CRE Daily (read). The HousingWire Divvy article returned 404.
- **Matic's "save an average of $970"** is a vendor claim.
- **Hippo partner API details** come from a third-party "API report card", not Hippo.
- **IRS 1031 deadlines:** the 45/180-day rule is cited through vendor pages (i1031); IRS.gov was not fetched.
- **Zillow renter research:** the 2025 Consumer Housing Trends renter page did not render. No renter survey statistics are included.
- **Blocked sources:** Capterra, Software Advice and GetApp (Cloudflare), BiggerPockets (Cloudflare), Inman (bot check), G2 and Reddit (skipped). Desktop and forum evidence therefore leans on Trustpilot and TrustRadius. Trustpilot pages may be unrepresentative: AppFolio's page notes "This company hasn't invited customers recently". Many Trustpilot reviews come from consumers or tenants rather than software buyers.
- **Trustpilot dates:** individual review dates were not captured from the page text. Quotes reflect the most recent reviews shown on 2026-10-08.
